- **R-MKT-1** A piece version is born a draft and is approved only by a person acting as themselves; no piece is approved, published or exported unless the brokerage legal name is rendered, no hard fair-housing term remains, every jurisdiction-pack rule passes, and the guard result names the content hash it was computed over, which must equal the piece's own (database-computed over prose, template, bound fields and ordered assets) hash; a guard result missing any of these is a refusal (R-GATE-12 extended to every piece). [DB, smoke]
- **R-MKT-2** Approved copy is frozen with its guard result and its images; any change is a new version, and the old one stays live until the new one is approved; a piece never changes listing, kind or version number. [DB, smoke]
- **R-MKT-3** Bound facts (price, status, beds, baths, sqft, address, open-house time, photos) render live from the team listing and never need re-approval; model-written copy may not contain price or date numerals. [core + api]
- **R-MKT-4** A brokerage identity or pack change re-renders live pieces; one that no longer passes is unpublished from pages we control within 1 h and needs a new version; its external publications (R-MKT-12) are retracted by the system after the provider confirmed the deletion, where the provider's API supports it (**VERIFY** per provider, incl. Instagram), otherwise a feed card tells the agent to remove the post themselves. [api + job]
- **R-MKT-5** Only the team's own listings (`source = 'team'`) get marketing pieces. [DB, smoke]
- **R-MKT-6** `exported` means handed to the agent; the product never says a post went live where it cannot verify it; Facebook Marketplace is export-only. [web copy lint + api]
- **R-MKT-7** A public listing page is served only through `app.public_listing_page` and returns the approved prose, the bound facts, the identity block and the piece's assets read through `marketing_piece_assets` only, never contact data; a retracted page or an off-market listing returns 410. [DB definer + api]
- **R-MKT-8** An open-house invite is email only, one gated `messages` row per recipient linked by `marketing_piece_id`, dispatched only while that piece is an approved or published open-house invite (DB guard); the send reports "Sent 41 of 45; 4 opted out" and blocked recipients do not raise `failed_send` (the exclusion lives in `deriveExceptions`, module 18a). An invite is a mass send, **never a touch** (R-OWN-14, D8): it records no first contact and counts in no scoreboard, goal or waiting-client check, exactly like a bulk send (b7 review sends/F1). A merged-away recipient is not sent to and is reported on its own ("1 merged into another record", naming the survivor to a caller who sees it; b7 review sends/F5). [DB guard + core + api, smoke]
- **R-MKT-9** When a listing goes sold, withdrawn or expired, unsent invites are canceled, the page says so and exports are flagged "may be out of date"; external publications are retracted where the provider supports it, otherwise a feed card tells the agent to remove the post themselves (as R-MKT-4). [api]
- **R-MKT-10** Marketing numbers come only from first-party facts (form submissions, sent invites) or provider-reported numbers labelled with their source; nothing is estimated and no page-view count is shown. [core + web]
- **R-MKT-11** Review requests go to every client the post-closing playbook covers, never filtered by sentiment, feedback or outcome, and never offer an incentive; they run only once `platform:review_requests` is cleared. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (FTC consumer-review rule, platform policies). [core]
- **R-MKT-12** (D2) Posting to Facebook Pages and Instagram business accounts (module 29) and to GBP locations (module 53's branch of the same file) goes only through `publishPost` (`marketing/publish.ts`): a person taps as themselves (TX1 inserts a `marketing_publications` row `publishing`); only the system records the provider's outcome (`published` with the provider's post id, `failed`, `unknown`) and a retraction (after the provider confirmed the deletion); `unknown` is reconciled by lookup, never re-posted blindly (one live row per piece and target); a social post goes only to `facebook_page`/`instagram_business`, a GBP post only to `gbp_location`, only to a `connected`/`degraded` connection and only once `platform:fair_housing` is cleared; otherwise export. The product says "Posted" only for a provider-confirmed row. A piece's `published` status means its public page is live or at least one of its publications is `published` (module 29 sets it after a confirmed publication). A new version never touches the old version's external posts (the UI says the earlier post stays on the Page); retracting one is the system's, after the provider confirmed the deletion, where the API supports it (**VERIFY**), otherwise a feed card tells the agent to remove it themselves. Exactly one retraction of a publication at a time asks the provider: a short system transaction claims it first (`retract_claim_id`), the provider call runs outside any transaction, and the outcome clears the claim; a concurrent request never calls the provider and never raises a "remove it yourself" card for a post the other request took down: the single-post form answers `PUBLICATION_RETRACTING`, take-down-all counts it as `inProgress`, and the reconcile pass leaves the post to the claim holder; the provider call is waited for at most 60 s; a claim whose process died is taken again after 5 minutes, and the reconcile pass finishes it whatever the listing; a confirmed deletion takes the post off any open "remove it yourself" card. Two concurrent taps for one piece and account make one live row and one provider call, and the second is refused `PUBLICATION_ALREADY_LIVE` (TX2 locks the piece before the publication row, the order TX1 takes them, so the two never deadlock). If a timed-out post cannot be found without a post id (**VERIFY**), `unknown` stays unknown and the UI says "Check your Page/Instagram". The Meta connection never requests messaging permissions and receives no webhooks (**VERIFY** permission names). [DB, smoke + api + lint]
- **R-FH-1** A hard fair-housing match blocks approval of a piece (DB CHECK on `guard_result`). [DB, smoke]
- **R-FH-2** Assistant output with a hard or soft fair-housing match is never autonomous: a step downgrades to a draft and the draft is flagged "Check this" (content guard, module 25). [core]
- **R-FH-3** The assistant (text and voice) never answers questions about who lives in an area, its safety or its "kind of people"; it hands back with a neutral note. [core + fixtures]
- **R-FH-4** Audience and targeting rules cannot reference protected classes, protected-proxy attributes, prediction output or (for ads) ZIP/neighborhood geography (type + runtime validator). **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** per jurisdiction. [core]
- **R-FH-5** The fair-housing lexicon is pack data with hard and soft terms and handles negation; its counsel-review state is the `platform:fair_housing` pack, and the UI states when it has not been reviewed. [core + web]
- **R-GBP-1** A review reply is a `review_reply_draft` that always needs a tap; it never repeats the client's name, the price or deal details (privacy guard: `apps/api/src/gbp/guard.ts`, pure; packages/core/src has no gbp), and it is posted only through `postReviewReply`. [api pure + api]
- **R-GBP-2** GBP posts are `gbp_post` marketing pieces under R-MKT-1/2 and are exported unless a connected, approved integration publishes them. D2: they publish only through `publishPost` to a `gbp_location`, the branch module 53 adds (R-MKT-12). [api]
- **R-GBP-3** Google Business Profile is gated by its own platform pack, platform:gbp (counsel review; not covered by D11); posts and replies also need platform:fair_housing. While platform:gbp is uncleared: the DB refuses storing a review, drafting a reply and publishing to a gbp_location; the API returns no stored review; the API makes no GBP provider call (sync, publish, lookup, reply), except deleting a live post whose listing must come down. N15 (API access) is gated separately by the unconfigured adapter and connection status. [DB + api, smoke]
- **R-GBP-4** A review reply is an owner-less review_reply_draft (owner_user_id, contact_id NULL): only Owners and Admins draft, approve or reject it (a support actor with a grant may read it), never through the generic action routes; every GBP route checks the action's kind and review. The approval is the claim: one provider call per approval, through sendReviewReply in marketing/publish.ts only; "Replied" only once confirmed or seen by the sync; a definite failure returns the draft to proposed, a lost answer is settled by the sync, never by re-posting. A draft whose review changed since drafting is refused until drafted again. The model sees only the rating, the review text and the team name; an assistant draft that fails the guard is not stored. [api + DB decision guard]
- **R-GBP-5** Reviews are public data about the business, not contact data: rating, text, the reviewer's display name, time, and whether the business replied. Never matched to a contact; never a touch, an inbound or a consent event; a reply is not a contact send (dispatch.ts not involved). Edited reviews are updated. A review missing from a complete single-pass sweep is blanked "Removed at Google" and its open draft invalidated; a failed, partial or unreadable pass removes nothing. [api + DB guard]

### Transactions and documents
- **R-TXN-1** A template- or document-proposed date drives nothing until a person confirms it; a non-manual confirmed date carries its confirmer, who acts as themselves; a `manual` confirmed date is entered, and any confirmed date is moved, only by a person (imports use source `import` through their definer). [DB, smoke]
- **R-TXN-2** Only confirmed, uncompleted dates drive reminders, risks and client/lender updates; `due_at` (end of the due day in `tenant_settings.default_timezone`, recorded in `basis`) and `reminder_at` are computed at confirm time by `core.confirmDate` and stored; module 31 is the only writer of deal dates (the Phase 1 deal-screen routes call it too) and its reconcile handler repairs a confirmed date whose `due_at` is missing; settings changes never move stored instants (R-OWN-27); completion is `completed_at` alone and clears the reminder. [core + api + DB]
- **R-TXN-3** Risks are R-ACC-6 `milestone_at_risk` exceptions derived by `deriveExceptions` over stored fields only, keyed `milestone:{deal_date}:{due_version}`; queue semantics are R-ACC-1/3. Deal-level risks (e.g. a financed deal with no lender contact) are not flagged; an unconfirmed date is never a risk. [core + job]
- **R-TXN-4** Client and lender updates are `deal_date_upcoming` steps of the approved team `transaction_updates` trigger playbook (template shipped by module 26; firings created by module 31's reminder reconcile handler from `reminder_at`): informational, confirmed dates only, dates as slots, recipients only the deal roles the step names, and of the client roles only the team's own side (K1, batch 8 review: `deals.side` buy → buyer, co_buyer; sell → seller, co_seller; dual → all four; lease → none, owner default: no automatic milestone updates on lease deals; lender, title and attorney exactly as the step names them), applied when the reminder is planned, re-read live when the update is built (`NOT_OUR_CLIENT`) and on the deal screen; any contract, money or wire wording downgrades to a draft. [core + api]
- **R-TXN-5** Moving a confirmed date bumps `due_version` and drops stored instants not recomputed in the same write, so firings and drafts bound to the old version go stale. A draft that is stale because its deal date moved, was completed or waived, or is gone says so in the web (the reason from `DRAFT_STALE` `details.reason`) and offers no re-send of the draft: no "Send anyway" and no re-tap in Approvals (Dismiss only), the compose box detaches the draft and keeps its text as the agent's own, and a feed card says why in place (backlog gaps, E557). [DB, smoke, web]
- **R-TXN-6** Milestone templates are tenant data per side and jurisdiction, shown as unreviewed until counsel reviewed them; no state's contract defaults are hard-coded. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** [core + web]
- **R-DOC-1** A document's visibility is its scope's: a deal document follows the deal; a listing document is for the listing's writers (listing agent, Team Lead, tenant-wide) and the members of a listing-side deal on it, never every member (a listing agreement carries commission terms and seller PII); `shared_to_portal` is an explicit staff action and only for clean deal/listing documents. [DB, smoke]
- **R-DOC-2** Extracted contract dates are proposals with page and character-range evidence; extraction is never autonomous (`contract`); it reads the PDF text layer only: a PDF without one says "No text found in this PDF; enter the dates by hand". [core + DB]
- **R-DOC-3** Every upload starts `pending` and unshared whatever the writer says; only the scan (the system) moves it out of `pending`, once (`infected` is final); a document is unreadable until clean except to its uploader and tenant-wide roles; quarantined objects are never streamed; a document's object is readable to staff only through a clean document they can read. [DB, smoke]
- **R-DOC-4** Documents are retained for the configured period after close, with legal hold; only a human Owner/Admin places or lifts a hold or shortens retention (audited); scope, file, uploader and version never change; a held or retained document cannot be deleted, so erasure and purge refuse (the sweep skips such contacts), and erasure shreds the contact's document objects. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (licence-law retention). [DB + retention, smoke]
- **R-DOC-5** Upload. Accepted types are PDF, JPEG and PNG, identified by their bytes (`%PDF-`, `FF D8 FF`, the PNG signature); the declared type must match the sniffed one, and an empty file or any other type is refused (`DOCUMENT_TYPE_NOT_ALLOWED`, E400). A file is at most 10,000,000 bytes (`DOCUMENT_TOO_LARGE`; a larger declared body is 413 before it is read, E401), sent as binary multipart: one `file`, a `kind` (not on versions) and an optional `displayName` of at most 1 KiB; a compressed body is refused before it is read; files are never decompressed and are stored byte-for-byte with their sha256, and the database re-binds the type and size (R-DOC-6). A person may make at most 60 upload attempts an hour. The display name is the file name without its path, control or bidi characters, cut to 200; it is never logged, never placed in a job payload and never audited. Uploads and new versions to a merged-away record are refused (`CONTACT_MERGED`, with `mergedInto` when the caller sees the survivor). [API + DB]
- **R-DOC-6** Integrity. A document names its uploader's own live, unshredded `document` object (PDF, JPEG or PNG, 1 to 10,000,000 bytes), used by no other document (`DOCUMENT_OBJECT_INVALID`, E408); the uploader is the acting person, so the system never uploads (`DOCUMENT_UPLOADER_SELF`); a new version supersedes one document the uploader can read, in the same deal, listing or contact (`DOCUMENT_VERSION_INVALID`, E407), and only once (a chain is linear: one successor per version, E406); the database sets `version_no = previous + 1` and keeps the previous kind. Referential checks bypass RLS, so the database checks this in an invoker trigger, not the foreign key (migration `0069_documents` `app.documents_integrity_guard`; the brief's lane number 0140). [DB]
- **R-DOC-7** Scan and download. The virus check runs only through the platform scanner (platform configuration, R-INT-1): with none configured (production until N11) every upload stays `pending` and nobody opens it, the Owner included, and the screens say why; only the system writes the verdict, once (a scanner error is retried and never makes a document clean; a pending scan is re-enqueued after 2 minutes by the per-tenant reconcile). Malware and unscannable (password-protected or corrupt) files are both blocked (`infected`, for good) and the object is shredded at once, unless the document is on legal hold, in which case it stays locked until the hold is lifted and is then shredded by the reconcile; a viewer who cannot read the verdict is still asked for a copy without a password (Q5). Only clean files stream: the query that fetches the key requires `scan_status = 'clean'` and a live object (the uploader and the Owner can read a quarantined object row, so the API, not RLS, refuses them: `DOCUMENT_QUARANTINED`, `DOCUMENT_BLOCKED`), with `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox` and PDFs always as attachments (an RFC 5987 UTF-8 file name). Staff downloads are audited by ids; Support lists metadata under a grant but never downloads. A test scanner says "This deployment uses a test virus scanner: files are not really checked." [API + jobs]
- **R-DOC-8** Contract dates. Only the text layer is read, and only from clean PDF purchase contracts and addenda of a deal that have no clean newer version, through `DocumentTextExtractor` and a deterministic labelled-date finder with built-in labels (not reviewed); no model reads contract text, and relative deadlines ("10 days after acceptance") and images are never read. Each candidate becomes a `date_time` claim with page and character-range evidence (a snippet of at most 160 characters). A claim is `needs_review` if its quote is not found verbatim at its page's character range or the snippet's own date is not the proposed day; the database never lets a `needs_review` claim become a date. The extraction (one `contract_extraction` action) belongs to its uploader and to tenant-wide actors; another member sees the dates once confirmed, and the generic Approvals routes never decide it (`EXTRACTION_USE_DOCUMENT`; the generic approve only acknowledges). A deal date is written only when a person confirms a claim, through module 31's `confirmInTx` (`fromDocument`: source `document`, the document basis, `evidence_claim_id`), from a verified claim of a `contract_extraction` of the same deal, at most one date per claim; the database refuses anything else (`DEAL_DATE_EVIDENCE_INVALID`). A new version that scans clean supersedes the older version's open extraction (its claims answer `DOCUMENT_SUPERSEDED`). [API + DB]
- **R-DOC-9** Sharing. Only a person who can write the deal or the listing (the deal's agent, the listing's writers, a Team Lead, the Owner or an Admin) may share or unshare one of its clean documents; TCs and ISAs never can, and contact documents are never shared (`DOCUMENT_NOT_SHAREABLE`; the database re-checks: `DOCUMENT_SHARE_NOT_ALLOWED`). Publishing to the portal is module 45's job; a new version is never shared automatically (the response says the previous version is still shared, E409). Unsharing stops client downloads at once (module 43's per-download re-check); retracting the client's portal item is module 45's (E410). **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL (C10):** sharing a listing agreement (commission terms) to a seller's portal; no client sees one before `platform:portal` is cleared. [DB + API]
- **R-DOC-10** Retention. At close, documents with no retention set get `closed_at + 7 years`; listing documents follow the latest closed sell or dual deal of the listing; contact documents are never stamped. The system never overwrites or shortens a retention date (it stamps only where `retention_until` is empty). An Owner or Admin may set retention between 3 and 10 years after the close (or the upload when there is none) and may place or lift a legal hold; both are audited with before and after. A retention date cannot be cleared once set (the body needs a date in range), so a retained contact document blocks erasure until the date passes (owner question, b11 ownership-data/F4). Nothing is purged after retention yet (a recorded gap, R-DATA-6). **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL (C9, C13):** 7 years after close within 3–10 by state transaction-file and licence law, and whether listing documents follow the sell-side close. [job + API]
- The ids R-DOC-5…10 have the brief's meanings (batch 11 reconciliation documents-1: the lane had numbered them itself; migration 0069's header still cites the lane's R-DOC-10 for the evidence guard, which is R-DOC-8 here). `docs/phase23/draft-b.md`'s e-sign "R-DOC-5" is not authoritative.

### Home values
- **R-AVM-1** At most one `ok` estimate per address per month, and only an `ok` estimate carries a value; every display shows the provider, the range, the as-of date and "an estimate, not an appraisal". **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** [DB + core]
- **R-AVM-2** A home-value step sends on its own only when `platform:avm` is cleared and the estimate is fresh, `display_allowed`, its range is within `avm_max_range_bps` and its change from the prior estimate is within the jump threshold; otherwise it becomes a draft or `skipped_ineligible` with the reason; it never says "unchanged" when no value was available. [core]
- **R-AVM-3** A valuation is requested only for a contact who can still receive the step (preview) at a current address, at most monthly per address (the `avm:{contact_address}:{yyyy-mm}` firing key) and at most monthly per person and house across a merge (every record of the person, the same normalized address; the prior estimate is read the same way). Only the team's buy-side clients qualify (K2, batch 8 review): buyer or co_buyer on a buy or dual deal closed before the month, never through the deal's primary contact alone, never a lease deal. The house is the purchased property: the deal's listing address when it is known and matches one of the contact's current addresses, otherwise the contact's current `home` address; never an address matching a property the person sold through the team (seller or co_seller on a closed sell or dual deal). Re-checked under the contact lock before the answer is stored and again when the note is built (`ADDRESS_NOT_CURRENT`). [api]

### Owner view, goals, calendar
- **R-OWNER-1** Owner-view figures are computed only in a human Owner/Admin transaction (the assistant and system never read `deal_financials`); every number shows its denominator, and deals left out (no commission, no source) are counted and listed. [api + web]
- **R-OWNER-2** Dollars by source sum commission of deals closed in the period by the deal's immutable `lead_source_id`; cost per closing with zero closings is said in words. [core]
- **R-OWNER-3** Velocity is median stage dwell with sample sizes from `app.stage_dwell_norms()` (no new view). The forecast covers the next 90 days in two units, closings and commission: for each stage, the share of deals that reached `closed` within 90 days of entering it over the trailing 365 days (k of n); expected value = the sum over open deals of their stage's share (times expected commission for the dollar unit); low/high = the same sum with the 80 % Wilson interval of each share; stages with n < 10 are left out and counted ("not enough history"). It returns `{low, high, horizonDays, unit, method, samples: {stage, n, k}[], excluded: {thinStages, noCommission, noSource}}`, with the method in one sentence; no per-deal probability. [core]
- **R-OWNER-4** No owner-view schema has per-deal probabilities or confidence fields (snapshot, R-AST-13 extended). [api]
- **R-OWNER-5** Source costs are read and written by a human Owner/Admin (manual rows, as themselves); the system reads and writes only `ads_sync` rows; a row's source, month and origin never change. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PLAN-1** A weekly plan is internal (`weekly_plan`, autonomy internal by CHECK): 3–5 items chosen by `planWeek(goals, pace, slowDeals, openPromises, apptsWithoutOutcome, tasks)` (pure), ranked by deadline first and then by the gap to the goal, each with evidence; accepting creates tasks; nothing is created without the agent's tap. [core + DB CHECK]
- **R-PLAN-2** Goal pace uses the scoreboard definers (0012). The touches method sentence names what the count holds: calls, texts, emails and website-chat replies the member sent themselves, never imported, bulk or the assistant's (D8(1); batch 7 review ownership-data/F7). [api]
- **R-PLAN-3** Items whose source the agent can no longer see are dropped at render; person-level do-not-contact contacts are never suggested. The do-not-contact is the whole person's (any record of it, along merges: `app.contact_person_ids`), A merge moves the person's tasks and appointments to the survivor (R-OWN-24), so the next plan offers them there; an item of a plan stored before the merge is shown and accepted on its evidence's current contact (the survivor) when the member sees that contact live and not do-not-contact, and every visible evidence row names it; otherwise it is dropped (b7 review ownership-data/F5; backlog gaps, E550, E551). [api + core]
- **R-CAL-1** Calendar links are visible only to their user and the system; Owners and Admins see nothing of a link. Like every audit row, its audit rows (connected, disconnected, revoked: provider and time, never a token or a calendar id) are theirs to read (owner decision D6, 2026-11-11). [DB, smoke]
- **R-CAL-2** Sync is one-way (app → calendar), fenced by `appointments.sync_version` (an older push never overwrites a newer one; `external_pushed_version` records what the calendar accepted); an edit made in the calendar is detected at the next push (etag mismatch) and the appointment is then detached, never overwritten, and the app says so; tokens refresh lazily inside the push. [api + DB]
- **R-CAL-3** Free/busy is read live per request and never stored. [api]
- **R-CAL-4** Revoking or disconnecting stops sync; in-app appointments are unaffected. [api]

### Gmail inbox sync (D2)
Read-only, per user, Phase 2 (modules 68 and 69). **smoke** in this subsection means `rls_smoke_gmail.sql` (187 checks).
- **R-GMAIL-1** Dark until cleared and switched on: links exist only while `platform:gmail` is cleared (`reviewed_by_counsel_at` and `rules.vendor_ready_at`) and `tenant_settings.mail_sync_enabled` is on; only the Owner turns it on, an Admin may turn it off; turning it off or the pack becoming uncleared hides every copy at once and revokes the links in the next reconcile pass (copy deletion: R-GMAIL-13). [DB, smoke]
- **R-GMAIL-2** Own mailbox only: a person links only their own mailbox (`users/me`) through the OAuth callback (the system actor, after reading the mailbox profile; the address is the profile's, and the callback stores its HMAC under the tenant HMAC key, `mailbox_address_hmac`, never changed); one live link per user; a mailbox is live in at most one link across **all** tenants (no shared mailbox, second teammate or second workspace); Owners, Admins and TLs see no link, token or sync state. [DB, smoke]
- **R-GMAIL-3** Least privilege: scopes exactly `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/gmail.readonly` (**VERIFY** that no narrower scope returns bodies; `gmail.metadata` returns none); no send, modify, labels or settings scope; nothing is ever sent, labeled, archived or deleted in Gmail; replies to clients go through `dispatchOutbound`/`canContact` (platform email) or happen in Gmail itself; a token granting more is refused by the callback and cannot be stored (CHECK). Gmail uses its own OAuth client (at least never `include_granted_scopes`), so a calendar grant never rides along and the exact-scope check holds (**VERIFY** Google's granted-scope semantics). Sending through Gmail: not built. [DB CHECK + api]
- **R-GMAIL-4** Tokens: only the refresh token is stored, envelope-encrypted (tenant field key, AAD = table/column/row id, `apps/api/src/crypto/envelope.ts`), decrypted only by the system actor inside a sync pass (module 68's architecture test forbids selecting `refresh_enc` outside `mailSync/token.ts`, and the API never returns it: the database cannot hide the column from the link's own user, because the system actor uses the same role, S-D2-7); access tokens live in memory only; revoking deletes the refresh token in the same transaction (CHECK) and revokes it at Google (best effort, retried); `purge_after` ≤ `revoked_at` + 30 days. [DB + api]
- **R-GMAIL-5** What is stored: a Gmail message only when one of its From/To/Cc/Bcc addresses (normalized, HMAC) equals a **current** email contact point of a live (not deleted, not merged) contact the mailbox owner owns, collaborates on (live) or shares a deal with, **that the mailbox owner confirmed for sync** (`mail_sync_points`: "Sync Gmail with these clients?" at connect, then a one-tap suggestion per new address; S-D2-1) and that is no live link's mailbox in the tenant (the agent's own or a teammate's Gmail is never a client address); a peer, a TL or the system cannot confirm for the agent; one row per (message, matched contact) with that contact's role only, the **count** of other recipients (never their addresses), the subject, the message's own plain text (quoted history and signature stripped best effort; HTML never stored; ≤ 20,000 characters, truncation flagged) and attachment metadata only (name, MIME type, size; ≤ 20; never fetched); never SPAM, TRASH, DRAFT or CHAT; at most `max_messages_per_thread` copies per thread per link (DB); bodies are plain text at rest like `messages.body` (**VERIFY** under N19 whether the security assessment expects field encryption). [DB guard + api]
- **R-GMAIL-6** Visibility: read exactly like the contact's Phase 1 timeline (`app.contact_detail_visible`) plus the mailbox owner for their own link's rows; never a client principal (Layer 1) and never a support actor (RESTRICTIVE, even under an Owner support grant; **VERIFY** Google API Services User Data Policy); unreadable the moment the link, the member, the switch or the pack stops being live, the thread is excluded, the contact point is removed or the mailbox owner withdraws the address's confirmation (`app.mail_copy_live`, S-D2-2, which answers only a caller these rules admit for that copy, so nobody else learns of a disconnect, an exclusion or a withdrawn confirmation); losing the relation (a reassignment) does not hide copies (E216). Visible to the whole timeline audience by default (owner question Q-GMAIL-5; the connect disclosure says so). [DB, smoke]
- **R-GMAIL-7** Private threads: only the mailbox owner (or the system) excludes a thread ("Remove this thread from Realty OS"); its copies are hidden at once and deleted within one sync interval, and later messages in it are never stored; an exclusion is final while the link lives (no un-exclude). There is no "hidden from the team but kept" mode. [DB, smoke]
- **R-GMAIL-8** Backfill: history at most `mail_backfill_days` (0–90, default 30, Owner-only; **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**) and at most `backfill_max_messages`; the origin is computed by the DB (live ⇔ Gmail `internalDate` ≥ `connected_at`); older history is refused; backfill never pauses automation or creates handbacks, and neither does a live message older than 1 hour at fetch (R-GMAIL-10); "import again" re-runs the window. [DB + api]
- **R-GMAIL-9** Opt-outs (D1): every Gmail message module 68 fetches **from** an address that matches a current email point in the tenant (live or backfill) runs the same deterministic classifier as platform inbound email (`classifyInbound`, channel `email`), **before and regardless of storage** (an excluded thread, the per-thread cap, an unrelated contact or an unconfirmed address stores nothing but still records the opt-out, S-D2-3, E229): an unsubscribe or keyword revokes email on that point; a wrong-number reply ("wrong person", checked before opt-out language) revokes email on that point with source `wrong_number`, exactly as platform email does (R-CONSENT-11; 0021's confirmed-wrong-number removal can cite it, R-PAR-12); opt-out language sets the person-level do-not-contact (`needs_review`) exactly as platform email does (R-CHAN-3, D-OPTOUT-LANG); module 68 classifies each message once; the consent event cites the stored copy (`{"kind":"synced_gmail","mail_message_id":…}`) or, when no copy is stored, the message (`{"kind":"synced_gmail","gmail_message_ref":"<sha256 hex of link id + Gmail message id>"}`), and survives the copy's deletion; `consent_events_synced_gmail` keys on either citation, so any event citing synced Gmail is a system email revoke (S-D2-5); synced mail never lifts an opt-out. [api + core + DB]
- **R-GMAIL-10** Fresh live inbound pauses: a live message from the contact whose Gmail `internalDate` is within 1 hour of the fetch runs `ingestSyncedMailInTx(tx, contactId, mailMessageId, classification)` (module 68's one flagged function in `comms/inbound.ts`): the Phase 1 lock, pause of enrollments, cancel of queued assistant messages and scheduled trigger runs, and exactly one handback "replied in Gmail" that cites the `mail_message` by id and time, with no body snippet and no `assistant.enrich_handback` job; it bumps the replying contact's conversation `inbound_seq` (only: never `last_inbound_at`, which would make a free-form send informational under R-CONSENT-5 and step around a marketing-only unsubscribe, D1), so a human message scheduled before the reply is held for a re-tap (E27) and a draft written before it is stale (R-AST-6 `DRAFT_STALE`) (b7 review); it writes no `messages` row and does not classify again (module 68 classified once with `classifyInbound`). The handback dedupe key is `email:{contact}:{rfc_message_id_sha256}` when the Message-ID hash is known (shared with platform email, R-GMAIL-12), else one per Gmail message. An older live message (found by a resync or after an outage) and every backfill message are stored and opt-out-classified (R-GMAIL-9) but neither pause nor hand back. [api]
- **R-GMAIL-11** Idempotent incremental sync: Gmail history from the stored `history_id`; unique (link, Gmail message, contact), so replays are no-ops; the cursor advances only after the page's rows commit; a message deleted, trashed or marked spam in Gmail deletes its copies; an expired history id triggers a bounded resync inside the backfill window, never a full-mailbox read (what it finds pauses only under R-GMAIL-10's 1-hour rule); polling: R-OWN-27; no Pub/Sub push in v1 (**VERIFY** Gmail API). [api]
- **R-GMAIL-12** Dedupe with platform email: `messages.rfc_message_id_sha256` holds the RFC 5322 Message-ID hash of platform email (inbound and outbound, written by module 22 as the system, once: trigger `messages_rfc_guard`); a Gmail copy with the same hash for the same contact (or a contact merged into it) is refused by the DB, and a copy that arrived first is deleted when the platform row lands (module 68 reconcile); the platform row wins. The DB refuses a duplicate only of platform email whose row carries the hash: rows saved before module 22 writes it, rows whose Message-ID module 22 could not learn and imported email (which never has one) are not deduplicated, so a backfill can show such a message twice; hence the rollout precondition in the `platform:gmail` notes (`vendor_ready_at` only after 90 days of hashing, or after a one-time repair). One handback: both paths use the handback dedupe key `email:{contact}:{rfc_message_id_sha256}` when the hash is known (module 22 on platform inbound, module 68 in `ingestSyncedMailInTx`), so whichever lands first hands back and the other does not. [DB + api]
- **R-GMAIL-13** Revocation, copy deletion, merge: a user disconnect, member removal (a suspension only pauses the link: R-INT-5), the switch turned off or the pack uncleared (R-GMAIL-1) and provider revocation (`invalid_grant`) revoke the link (the token: R-GMAIL-4). Its copies are hidden at once (RESTRICTIVE `mail_messages_live`) and deleted by module 68's reconcile handler within one sync interval (the same pass deletes copies whose contact point was removed or whose confirmation was withdrawn, S-D2-2) (`poll_interval_seconds`, 120 s by default); that handler also selects revoked links that still have copies, not only links whose `next_sync_at` is due. The link and its exclusions are purged at `purge_after` (≤ 30 d); database backups expire per DEPLOY backup retention (DEPLOY §8: point-in-time window of at least 35 days; **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**). Erasure: R-DATA-7. Merge: when two contacts are merged, the system re-points the merged contact's copies to the survivor and to the survivor's email point with the same address HMAC, when the mailbox owner confirmed that point (`mail_messages_update_guard`; only `contact_id` and `contact_point_id` change); a copy that would collide with the survivor's own copy of the same Gmail message, or whose survivor point is unconfirmed, is deleted instead (the survivor's address then appears as a suggestion). Otherwise copies are kept while the link lives (**VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**). [DB + retention, smoke]
- **R-GMAIL-14** Never a channel, never in a model: synced mail never becomes a `messages` row (a fresh reply only moves the conversation's inbound counter, R-GMAIL-10), never dispatches, never counts as a touch, speed-to-lead or ownership event, and is never sent to an AI model or used in assistant drafts (**VERIFY** Google Limited Use for AI); evidence cites it by id and time (`evidence_refs.source_type = 'mail_message'`, never quoted: `evidence_refs_mail_not_quoted`); it is read only through module 68's `GET /contacts/:id/mail-messages` (RLS does the visibility); no SQL names `mail_messages` or `user_mail_links` outside `apps/api/src/mailSync/**` (plus `ingestSyncedMailInTx` in `comms/inbound.ts`) and `mailSync/**` imports no model provider (architecture test); link, disconnect, exclusion, import-again, the Owner switch and window changes are audited without content. Because a reply sent from Gmail never stops a first-contact deadline, the product says so: the connect disclosure, and the mailbox owner's own Gmail entry in the thread while that contact's first-contact deadline is open ("Replies you send from Gmail don't count as first contact. Reply here to stop the clock."; batch 7 review sends/F4). The compose box counts a newer synced Gmail from the contact as "wrote last by email" (it never says they wrote last by text under it; ui/F1), and a merged-away record shows no synced Gmail (its copies move to the current record with the next sync pass; ui/F6). [lint + architecture test + api]

### Coaching
- **R-COACH-1** Only calls with a transcript and a recording basis of `announced_all_parties` or `one_party_policy` are reviewed, and only while the Owner turned call scoring on (which needs `platform:coaching` cleared); imported recordings never are. [api + DB]
- **R-COACH-2** A rubric item is met or missed only with a verified transcript quote; otherwise it is `needs_review` and leaves the denominator; the UI shows "6 of 8 checks", never a bare score. [core + DB deferred trigger]
- **R-COACH-3** A call review covers a call a person handled (never an AI-handled call), for the agent and contact of that call, and is visible only to that agent, Owners/Admins and, only when the Owner turned on `tl_sees_call_scores`, that agent's Team Lead, and only while the caller may play the call's recording. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (employee monitoring). Only roles that take call reviews (`coaching.view`: Owner, Admin, Team Lead, agent, ISA; never a TC) have calls reviewed or a daily fix: `core.reviewEligible` answers `role_not_reviewed` for a TC's call, and the scan and the daily-fix reconcile skip TCs, so a tip card never opens a page closed to its holder (batch 7 review ui/F5; core + api, not a DB check). [DB, smoke]
- **R-COACH-4** At most one daily fix per agent per local day: the item missed most often in the last 10 scored calls (minimum 3), never one dismissed in the last 7 days; no data ⇒ no card. [core]
- **R-COACH-5** Call scores never feed routing, ownership, prediction, the scoreboard or any automatic consequence. [lint + architecture test]
- **R-COACH-6** No numeric overall score, grade, leaderboard, voice-tone or sentiment analysis is computed or shown. [snapshot test]
- **R-COACH-7** The weekly improving/slipping view compares the same rubric version, shows n on each side and needs ≥ 5 scored calls per window; a TL sees aggregates only (no quotes, call or contact ids). [core + DB definer]
- **R-COACH-8** app.coaching_trends answers only a staff user who takes call reviews (never support, the system, a client or a TC). Scope: their own trend; any member's for an Owner or Admin; a Team Lead's team members only while the Owner's tl_sees_call_scores is on (default, owner question 3); any other id refuses the whole call. It returns per-item counts over fixed whole local weeks (1-4 against as many before), one rubric version, reviews whose recording still exists; met is withheld below 5 scored calls and wherever any (member, week) cell inside has 1-4, so overlapping answers reveal nothing about fewer than 5 of someone else's calls. Only while platform:coaching is cleared and call scoring is on. Computed on request, never stored. [DB definer + api]

### Voice answering (Phase 3)
- **R-VOICE-1** The assistant only answers inbound, client-initiated calls, under an approved `voice_answering` version; no AI-originated or AI-handled outbound call can exist. [DB, smoke]
- **R-VOICE-2** For an AI call, processing (`answered_at`) starts only after the self-identifying disclosure and the recording announcement both played, whatever the call's final status, and every tool request comes after both; a failed announcement ends the AI leg to voicemail (`fallback_voicemail`, never answered). **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** [DB, smoke]
- **R-VOICE-3** It answers only after the ring-through fails (`voice_answer_after_seconds`) or outside routing hours per `voice_answer_when`; the takeover is the telephony dial timeout decided at call start and stored (`ai_takeover_at`), not a sweeper timer. [core + db]
- **R-VOICE-4** Every tool request is decided by `core.voice.decideToolCall` against the closed set and the approved playbook and logged append-only, with arguments limited to a per-tool allowlist of ids, codes and slot instants (no free text); a denied request has no effect. [core + DB, smoke]
- **R-VOICE-5** Scope is qualify, book, take a message, warm-transfer; price, offers, contracts, commission, legal, lending and neighborhood-demographic questions become a message for the agent. [core + fixtures]
- **R-VOICE-6** Voice answering never changes ownership. A live transfer goes only to the contact's current owner, when the owner is accepting leads and answers within the hold (`voice_transfer_hold_seconds`, a pack value, default 20 s); every other caller (unowned, after hours, owner unavailable) is booked into the owner's calendar or leaves a message, and a critical handback goes to the owner or, for an unowned caller, to whoever the normal intake offer reaches. [core + db]
- **R-VOICE-7** Every AI call ends in exactly one handback with transcript evidence; unverified details are needs-review and never become appointment times; a completed AI call without a handback after 10 min is a P1 alert. [api + job]
- **R-VOICE-8** Asked whether it is a person, it answers truthfully; it never claims to be human or uses a real person's voice. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (state AI-disclosure laws; FCC treatment of AI voices). [fixtures + content guard]
- **R-VOICE-9** Voice answering ships dark: an AI-handled call needs the Owner-enabled, attested setting in the active compliance policy and the `platform:voice` pack cleared (counsel and the vendor latency spike); only the Owner turns it on, including by re-activating an older voice-on policy version; turning it off is open to Admins. [DB, smoke]
- **R-VOICE-10** Vendor failure or latency beyond the pack threshold falls back to voicemail; the caller is never left in silence. [api]
- **R-VOICE-11** Takeover timing: decided once at call start and stored (ai_takeover_at for a yes; recording_decision.voiceTakeover {at, code} for both), replayed on retries. With a takeover planned the Dial timeout is voice_answer_after_seconds. The DB guard decides at the takeover instant; a refusal is the Phase 1 voicemail, unchanged. [api]
- **R-VOICE-12** No takeover when ai_enabled is false, the agent is unconfigured, the fake agent meets real telephony, TELEPHONY_LIVE_REQUESTS is off on real telephony (O5; refusal TELEPHONY_LIVE_OFF), or the active policy has recording off (O13). [api]
- **R-VOICE-13** Caller ID is not identity: the model's context holds no contact data, only the approved content, enabled ledger tools, offered slots when allowed and fixed lines. [api + type test]
- **R-VOICE-14** Ledger: every closed-set entry after both announcements is written with core's seq before its effect; entries the DB cannot hold are listed in the handback by seq, reason and time; at most 200 rows, then the session ends to voicemail through core. [api + DB]
- **R-VOICE-15** One handback per AI call, written with voice_outcome, transfer_outcome and handback_action_id in one transaction, merging a ring-time or recovery handback. Claims are code-built and quote-free; the caller's words live only in the encrypted transcript. Critical unless transferred live. [api]
- **R-VOICE-16** Only calendar bookSlot books, as the system, under the owner's lock; an appointment committed after the session ended is canceled in the end transaction. [api]
- **R-VOICE-17** The booking confirmation is never sent automatically: a one-tap draft on core's confirmationChannel, sent through dispatchOutbound with the D1 re-check. [api]
- **R-VOICE-18** A spoken opt-out runs planInboundEffects on 'voice' for the caller's address, citing evidence_call_id and the offset; person scope calls app.record_person_opt_out. Opt-out language has the same person-level outcome as by SMS; a keyword revokes voice on that address. [api]
- **R-VOICE-19** Transfer is TwiML only, to the owner's verified alert phone, via the existing screen, accept and dial-result paths; hold = Dial timeout; the owner presses 1; transfer_outcome is written once; no new path, REST call or number configuration. [api + contract test]
- **R-VOICE-20** Enablement: on = Owner + re-auth + the voice attestation, with an approved version, a cleared pack, a configured agent and recording on; the new version copies every column of the active one (drift-tested). Off = Owner, or Admin unless the active version is attested. Widening the timing is Owner-only (API). [api + DB]
- **R-VOICE-21** voice.reconcile: an AI call without a handback 10 min after it ended, or maxCallSeconds + 10 min after the takeover, gets a recovery handback and a P1 alert (reason VOICE_HANDBACK_MISSING); idempotent. [job]
- **R-VOICE-22** An AI call is never a touch, first contact, speed-to-lead event or ownership change. [api + architecture]
- **R-VOICE-23** The hold is the pack's rules.voice_transfer_hold_seconds when an integer 5-60, else the content's transferHoldSeconds; the lane never publishes a pack version. [api]
- **R-VOICE-24** No hot-lead rule ships: production passes hotRule null, so nobody is transferred live; only tests inject one. [api + architecture]

### Prediction (Phase 3)
- **R-PRED-1** Only features of an approved, counsel-reviewed platform model enter a prediction; a model's features are a subset of the allowed property and first-party features (`years_in_home`, `equity_band`, `recent_saves`, `stated_timeline_days`, `past_client_years`, `recent_permit`), every reason cites a feature of its own model, and the feature type cannot express protected classes, protected proxies, names, demographics, household composition, geography or life events. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** [core types + DB, smoke]
- **R-PRED-2** Public-record data is limited to property facts by construction (no person-level column exists); attaching a record needs an exact address and owner-name match. [DB + core]
- **R-PRED-3** Output is a band with reasons and evidence, shown with the model's platform back-test ("of N people flagged in the back-test, M transacted within 90 days"); an approved model always carries one; no probability or confidence appears in any agent-facing schema. [DB + snapshot test]
- **R-PRED-4** Predictions only create feed cards for people; they never feed messages, routing, ownership, pricing, ads or the portal (architecture test). [lint + test]
- **R-PRED-5** Withdrawing a model hides every score computed with it, and every feed card made from them, at once. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PRED-6** Erasure removes scores and property records; person-level do-not-contact contacts are never listed for outreach. [DB + core]

### Homeowner profile (owner decision D10; modules 77-79)
Numbered as the lane's brief numbers them (batch 11 reconciliation homeowner-1). Migration `0074_homeowner_profiles.sql` and the lane's history use the lane's first numbering, in which 5 was the parser (now in 3 and 4), 6 connector safety (now 10), 7 system writes and refresh (now 6 and 7), 8 reads and the view (now 7, 4 and 5) and 10 "nothing sends" (now 8).
- **R-HOME-1** **Recognition.** A profile is built only for a live contact with at least one recorded reason, computed by the system each night from data already on the record: seller intent (`seller` or `buyer_seller`); a `sell` or `value` inquiry of any age (the panel shows the latest one's date, owner question 16); a configured homeowner tag (the platform tag keys, "Homeowner" by default) that is not `protected_proxy`; a past buyer (K2, R-AVM-3: from the month after the closing). "Live" means not deleted, merged away, closed or do-not-contact. Losing recognition stops lookups and purges the profile on the next pass. Nothing hooks intake, tags or deals. A merge moves tags and deal roles but no addresses, inquiries or intent (0027, 0053), so recognition restarts from the survivor's own record and the survivor builds its own profile from its own subjects; the merged-away record's profile is purged (R-HOME-9). When the survivor is recognised, its panel tells every viewer of the survivor (its agent, its Team Lead, Owner and Admin) that a record was merged into it and that the profile uses only the survivor's own addresses, inquiries and intent; the flag is read from the survivor's merge tree (`app.contact_merge_tree_ids`, 0075: ids only), because contacts RLS hides the closed, unowned merged-away record from the agent and Team Lead (batch 11 review ownership-data/F6). An unrecognised survivor shows no panel, like any unrecognised contact: the merged-away record's recognition does not carry over, and the panel does not say that record had one (decided in the batch 11 review; owner question whether a short line, or recognition over the merge tree, should replace this). [api + core]
- **R-HOME-2** **Subjects and input.** Subjects are K2's purchased house (an address of any kind), then complete home and property addresses, ordered K2, home, property, oldest first, at most 3. Otherwise mailing and interest addresses are never subjects. The county comes from a platform table (region SC or "South Carolina" and listed five-digit ZIPs; **VERIFY**): Beaufort County has a connector, Jasper County links only, and a ZIP shared by both is tried at Beaufort. The adapter gets exactly one postal address. Homeowner data comes only from `lookupHomeowner`, and module 50's `lookup` stays property-only. [core + api + architecture test]
- **R-HOME-3** **Owner match.** Values are stored only when core `attachPropertyRecord` matches the exact normalized address and one of the contact's names, after entity owners (an LLC, a trust, an estate, a bank; **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**, owner question 9) are dropped and each "A & B" owner is split. Otherwise only the status (`not_matched` with its detail, `not_available`, `ambiguous`, `failed`), the detail and the date are kept. The county answer is parsed field by field: at most ten owner names, and every key outside the closed field list (age, phone, email, lender, recorded instruments, co-owners' addresses, a balance or an estimate) is dropped. Nothing about another owner is stored, hashed or logged; a profile leaves `matched` only after its facts are deleted, and a fact exists only under a `matched` profile (DB guards). [DB guard + core + api]
- **R-HOME-4** **Provenance.** Every value has a source (NOT NULL, the provider pattern) and an as-of date, or the check date, shown as "read on", on every value. The parser drops a value with no valid source, a date outside 1800 to today + 1, cents that are not safe integers ≥ 0 and a string that is over-long or carries control or bidi characters. The kept values are a closed list: no loan balance, equity, estimate, score, confidence or owner name is representable in the database, the core types or the panel's response. Years owned names its source: the county's transfer date, or, when the record has none, the team's closing of the purchase that the viewer can see (E582). [DB + core + web]
- **R-HOME-5** **Honest absence.** A missing value says "Not available", why, and where to look. Links come from a code table (the county pages, each **VERIFY**) and never carry the person's address. Nothing is estimated; recorded loans have no automated source, and the panel links to the Register of Deeds. The D9 slots (MLS sale history, the in-house value) are "pending": batch 10 built D9's MLS sold data and core/valuation, but this panel does not read them yet, so they say "Not shown on this panel yet" (batch 11 review ownership-data/F8, api-ui/F7); wiring them is the D9 follow-up, and showing the in-house value to agents is verify_items 12 (**VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**). Jasper is links only. A recognised person with no subject is asked for the home address (E560); an address with no county says "No county source for this address" (E577). A test public-records source (the dev and staging-demo fake) says on every home that no county was asked (batch 11 review api-ui/F5). [core + web]
- **R-HOME-6** **Refresh.** Nightly per tenant (`homeowner.refresh`, hourly at :41, acting in the local night 02:00-05:00), with a per-local-date lookup budget (100 by default). A subject is due when never checked, 30 days after its last answer, after an address edit, or 1 day after an unavailable or timeout failure (a rejected or invalid address after 30 days or an edit). Lookups run outside transactions, and each write is fenced on the address (unchanged `updated_at`, same contact) and on recognition (still recognised, live, not do-not-contact). A failure keeps the old values, `checked_at` and retention and is shown; failures with no success raise a P2 alert. Unconfigured or live-off means no lookup and no row (the purge still runs). The pass writes only the two homeowner tables: never contacts, addresses, tags, deals, intent, stage or ownership, and the county mailing address is never copied to the contact. [api]
- **R-HOME-7** **Internal only.** The tables are staff-only (Layer 1: the client principal reads nothing), readable exactly by the viewers of the contact's detail (`contact.view_detail`, RLS), written only by the system, and read-only for support (support reads under an Owner's grant and writes nothing: RLS and the statement trigger). A past buyer's closing date, and years owned from it, are shown only to a viewer who can see the deal. No portal, message, template, document, assistant, AI, nurture, marketing, seller-update, list, filter, search, bulk or export code reads them (architecture test; core modules included), and the panel says it is internal and never shown to the client. [DB + architecture test]
- **R-HOME-8** **No outreach.** No value triggers, addresses or personalises any message, call, mail, task, card, notification or enrollment (architecture test). Future use needs `platform:public_records_outreach` cleared by its own counsel-review command on that deployment (the pack covers SC only, `{"states":["SC"]}`) and its own lane; the clearance is recorded per deployment, never as a code default, and the panel says whether it is recorded. Other states are **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**. [architecture test + pack]
- **R-HOME-9** **Retention.** At most 12 months after the last answered check (a 366-day DB CHECK); hidden at expiry (the select policy) and purged nightly, even while the provider is not configured. Also purged on lost recognition, do-not-contact, closing, merge or deletion of the contact, and for a mailing or interest address that is no longer K2's. Erasure and address deletion cascade. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** [DB + api]
- **R-HOME-10** **Connector safety.** No request unless `PUBLIC_RECORDS_LIVE_CALLS=true` (default false everywhere; while false no request is built). The county connector fills module 50's one `PublicRecordsProvider` (R-INT-1), and its property `lookup` always answers not configured, so module 50 never queries a county (R-PRED-2). GET only, to https service URLs that are code constants checked at start-up (default port, no credentials, query or fragment, host on `PUBLIC_RECORDS_ALLOWED_HOSTS`), with the where clause built only from whitelisted address tokens (an address that fails them is refused before any request). Size (2 MiB), time (15 s) and rate (at least 1 s apart per process, so the rate multiplies with worker processes; at most 3 requests per lookup) are bounded. No redirects, no credentials, and logs carry counts and codes only. [api + config]

### Ads (Phase 3)
D2: Google Ads only (`ads_google`); Meta ads are out of scope and `ads_meta` is never offered. Every R-ADS rule applies to Google Ads unchanged.
- **R-ADS-1** The ad cap is an append-only version inserted only by the Owner, as themselves, with an attestation. [DB, smoke]
- **R-ADS-2** Every planned budget change cites the cap version in force and an `ad_reallocation` an Owner or Admin approved, as themselves, that names exactly this (campaign, new daily budget); an approval moves each campaign once; at commit, under a per-tenant lock, the tenant-wide daily total (this run's plan, every other campaign's latest planned or applied budget, paused campaigns at 0) never exceeds that version's daily ceiling. Auto-applying shifts is not built (owner decision D-ADS). [DB, smoke]
- **R-ADS-3** Budget changes and pauses go only through `ads/dispatch.ts` (the reserved names `applyBudgetChange` and `pauseCampaign`, R-GATE-15): planned ledger row first, provider apply with the event id as idempotency key, then applied/failed rows that must match the planned row's run, campaign and amount; a campaign's budget mirror is written only by the system. [lint + api + DB, smoke]
- **R-ADS-4** Every campaign declares the housing special category and passes the targeting validator; an active campaign runs only an approved or published creative of its own listing; no contact-list audiences, lookalikes or prediction output. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL / platform policy.** [DB + core, smoke]
- **R-ADS-5** Hourly reconciliation pauses every campaign once provider-reported month-to-date spend reaches the monthly cap and alerts the Owner; resuming is a tap; the UI says platforms may overspend a daily budget on a given day. [job + core + web]
- **R-ADS-6** A proposal below the minimum sample (5 agreements per campaign in 90 days) is "hold", with the counts shown. [core]
- **R-ADS-7** `platform:ads` ships published and unreviewed (owner decision D7); no `ad_reallocation` enters `approved` and no planned ledger row is written until `app.platform_cleared('platform:ads', true)` (counsel review AND `rules.vendor_ready_at`). It is not part of owner decision D11: no migration, seed or code default sets either field. Applied, failed and paused rows and `executed` / `trigger_ref` updates are never gated, so a run started while cleared can always finish. Until cleared, the API proposes and approves nothing and makes no provider write (no budget change, pause or re-apply; the cap pause re-checks the pack before each call); reads continue (Q-ADS-1, `READS_WHILE_DARK`); at the cap the reconcile only alerts, and GET /ads says the pause is off. [DB, smoke + api]
- **R-ADS-8** A planned row needs a campaign the caller sees on an `ads_google` connection (`ADS_GOOGLE_ONLY`, D2) that is connected or degraded (`ADS_CONNECTION_NOT_LIVE`); a campaign the caller cannot see is refused (fail closed). Nothing mirrors, proposes or pauses an `ads_meta` connection (D2). [DB + core + api, smoke]
- **R-ADS-9** Approval path. An `ad_reallocation` is created only by api/ads (the weekly proposal or hold, and the resume tap), with no owner and no contact: Owner, Admin, the system and a support session (read-only) read it; Team Leads and agents never do, and it reaches no one's assistant inbox or feed. It is approved only by `POST /ads/proposals/:id/approve` or the resume tap (`POST /ads/campaigns/:id/resume`), each approving and planning in one transaction (Owner/Admin, one tap; `reject` is the person's decision); the generic assistant approve and reject routes answer 404. An action approved any other way is never planned (`ADS_APPROVAL_NOT_FROM_ADS`). After approval its payload, edited_payload and kind are frozen (`AD_APPROVAL_FROZEN`). A new cap version or a moved amount makes a pending proposal stale (`ADS_PROPOSAL_STALE`, superseded). A hold is a `blocked` action, never planned. A connection without a lead source moves no money (`ADS_NO_LEAD_SOURCE`, b11 sends-gates/F2). [api + DB]
- **R-ADS-10** Apply outcomes. A timeout or an unreadable answer is unknown and writes nothing. The hourly readback decides: the planned amount ⇒ applied (`READBACK`); the from amount while the plan is still the campaign's latest event ⇒ one re-apply (marked first in `trigger_ref.reapplied`, only while cleared); another amount ⇒ failed `PLATFORM_CHANGED`; still unresolved after 24 hours ⇒ failed `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`. A pack withdrawn after TX1 ⇒ failed `PACK_WITHDRAWN` with no call. Never a second planned row per approval; TX2 never undoes a pause recorded since the plan. [api + DB]
- **R-ADS-11** Spend sync writes only `ads_sync` `lead_source_costs` rows: one per lead source and account-zone month (the current and the previous month recomputed, earlier months kept), updated in place and never deleted; the note names the zone and the sync time; manual rows are never read or written. The owner view sums both origins, and the ads page warns when one source has both for a month (E451). [api + RLS]
- **R-ADS-12** The campaign mirror records what Google Ads reports and recomputes each campaign's status every pass: removed ⇒ `ended`; a serving issue ⇒ `rejected`; paused ⇒ `paused`; enabled ⇒ `active` only when linked to a listing with a currently approved or published creative of it, the housing category declared, its own budget and US dollars; otherwise `draft` ("not managed here"), never moved. api/ads, as the system, writes the external id, status, targeting and the budget mirror (the DB enforces the budget, `AD_BUDGET_THROUGH_LEDGER`; the architecture test the rest); a person only links a campaign to a listing and an approved or published creative of it. `campaign_ref` = `gads:<campaign id>`. An unmanaged campaign's spend counts toward the monthly cap, its budget not toward the daily ceiling (both stated on GET /ads). Without the connection's lead source no campaign is added, but rows already mirrored are still updated. Nothing is written back to Google Ads except budget changes and pauses through `ads/dispatch.ts`. **VERIFY** the Google Ads report shapes. [api + DB]
- **R-ADS-13** The Google Ads connect requests exactly `GOOGLE_ADS_SCOPES` (`https://www.googleapis.com/auth/adwords`, **VERIFY**) and completes only through the OAuth callback's proof of control (E160). Production without a real, checked adapter gets `UnconfiguredAds` ("Google Ads is not set up on this platform"): it reads and changes nothing. Limits are honest states: `degraded` `ADS_QUOTA` (retried hourly), `degraded` `ADS_CURRENCY_UNSUPPORTED`, `revoked`. Spend unchecked for 2 hours alerts every active Owner and Admin, once per connection and day, for a live connection and for a revoked or disconnected one that still has mirrored campaigns, unless its account is connected again (E454); GET /ads's `uncheckedSentence` reads the same rule (`ads/unchecked.ts`). GET /ads's over-delivery note says what the cap does on this deployment: while dark it only alerts (or, with Q-ADS-1 answered no, reads nothing); cleared with no live connection it reads and pauses nothing (pause in Google Ads); with Q-ADS-3 answered no it counts only linked campaigns and pauses only managed ones, and the month-to-date sentence says the cap counts only those; with Q-ADS-1 answered no, campaigns and spend read earlier stay listed and the page says when they were read and that they are not updated (b11 api-ui/F4, ads-7). Every figure on GET /ads has its denominator and "as reported by Google Ads at …", and no confidence; no sentence points at a control the web app does not have. [api]

### Client portal (Phase 3)
- **R-PORTAL-1** A client principal reads zero rows from every table and view outside the portal allowlist and can never write. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PORTAL-2** A client sees only its own active account, its unrevoked and unexpired grants (lapse is immediate) on a deal or listing its contact is a party to (checked when the grant is made and on every read), and the unretracted items of those grants, never another deal, listing, account or tenant. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PORTAL-3** Portal items are published copies from client-visible sources of the grant's own deal or listing (confirmed milestones, clean shared documents, showing dates, feedback and seller updates a person shared, client-visible next steps); feedback and seller updates are never rule-published; an item never changes or moves, and its retraction is final. [DB CHECK + trigger + core + api, smoke]
- **R-PORTAL-4** Staff and client sessions are disjoint: separate cookie (`__Host-psid`), resolver, CSRF secret and transaction helper; a staff cookie on a portal route, or the reverse, is 401. [api + lint]
- **R-PORTAL-5** Clients sign in with a single-use magic link (hashed, 15 min) sent through `dispatchOutbound` (opt-outs apply) to the account's login point, which is one of the client's own current emails or phones (composite FK + trigger); sign-in waits for `platform:portal`; the request never reveals whether an address is on file. [api + DB, smoke]
- **R-PORTAL-6** Revoking a grant, removing the client from the deal or listing, or disabling the account hides everything on the next query; revocation and disabling are final (invite again). [DB, smoke]
- **R-PORTAL-7** Client document downloads go through a definer that re-checks the client policy, the item, the document's scope, `shared_to_portal` and `scan_status`, and a 60 s single-use stream token, and are audited. [api + DB definer]
- **R-PORTAL-8** Boundary. `/api/portal` is one encapsulated plugin: client-session routes in `portal/client/**` reach the DB only through `withPortalTx` and the portal wrappers, pre-session routes in `portal/signin/**`, staff routes in `portal/staff/**` under the staff guard; every route is marked; capability path segments never reach an access log. [api + lint]
- **R-PORTAL-9** Session and CSRF. `__Host-psid` httpOnly, Secure, SameSite=Strict, Path=/; each sign-in is a new session ending the presented one; unsafe client routes need a CSRF token under a portal-only derived key; staff credentials never authenticate portal routes, nor the reverse. [api]
- **R-PORTAL-10** Sign-in request. One 202 for every slug, address and account state, with lookup and delivery after the response; 429 only from limits that apply whatever the address; 503 for every address while uncleared, and for every address of a channel that cannot really send outside tests. [api]
- **R-PORTAL-11** The sign-in link. A system, informational `portal_login_link` message to the login point through `dispatchOutbound`; opt-outs, suppressions, do-not-contact and the DB guard apply, the automation pause does not; the stored body never holds the link (it is sealed in `portal_link_seals` and rendered only at the provider send, b14 audit F2); a link queued 15 minutes is canceled; never handed to a fake outside tests. [core + api + DB]
- **R-PORTAL-12** Rate limits. DB-backed per IP, address and IP+address on sign-in and session opens, plus the definer's account throttle; per-session limits on reads and stream tokens; small bodies on public portal routes. Per tenant (b14 review R1): sign-in link texts may use only their own share of the team's daily text cap (R-TWL-2), so the public route can never spend the team's budget. [api + DB]
- **R-PORTAL-13** Invite. A writer of the deal or listing, for a party only; one live account per contact with an active login point; one live grant per account and scope, serialized on the contact; the notice is the inviter's own message, tokenless, not sent while uncleared; idempotent. [api + DB]
- **R-PORTAL-14** Publish, retract, revoke, disable. A person as themselves, `publishable()` before the DB; feedback, seller updates, documents, showings and next steps only by a person; each final for its row; a retraction records its person (none for the sync); a retracted source may be published again. [core + api + DB]
- **R-PORTAL-15** `portal.sync`. Per tenant every 5 minutes as the system: publishes confirmed milestones of live deal grants only, never a key with any row nor a milestone a person retracted; retracts moved, waived or removed milestones, unshared documents, finished next steps, and a deal grant's showings, feedback and seller updates reached through the deal's listing once its contact is no longer a seller or co-seller (R-PORTAL-19; b14 review R3); stores expiry once, 90 days after the later of closing and the grant. [api + DB]
- **R-PORTAL-16** Client view. Only the three portal tables, only portal ids; the staff preview renders the same view and says why the client cannot see it now. [api]
- **R-PORTAL-17** Downloads. A 60 s single-use stream token plus the session, redeemed by `portal.document_download`, which re-checks the chain and returns that object's wrapped key version; decrypted in the API with the platform KEK; no staff or system transaction; attachment, nosniff, sandbox CSP, no-store. [api + DB definer]
- **R-PORTAL-18** Grant display copy. Title, scope and contact card are a published copy in `portal_grants.display` (allowlisted keys, lengths), written by a writer or the system, never a client, never a personal phone. [api + DB]
- **R-PORTAL-19** Seller-side binding. Through the deal's listing, a showing, its feedback or a seller update reaches a deal grant only for a seller or co-seller of that deal in `deal_contacts`, in core and the DB (at publish); after a role correction `portal.sync` retracts such items as the system within one pass (`NOT_SELLER_SIDE`, b14 review R3). [core + DB + api]

### Texting reactions (D5b)

- **R-REACT-1** An inbound text is a phone's reaction text only when it arrives as SMS without media and its whole body, normalised (NFC; U+200B, U+200C, U+2060, U+FEFF removed; whitespace collapsed), is exactly one quote form of the template table in `core/compliance/reactions.ts` (iPhone en-US and es-419 tapbacks, iPhone emoji and sticker forms, Google Messages en and es emoji forms; add and remove) with nothing before or after; media reactions are not parsed, and web chat, email, calls, MMS with media, imported history and agent alert phones never carry reactions. [core + api]
- **R-REACT-2** A reaction is linked to the team's text only when, under the classifier's normalisation with double quotes folded, its quote equals the whole body of a non-imported outbound `sms`/`mms` message on a record of the same person (the contact the reaction landed on, or another record merged with it: between a Team Lead's merge and its follow-up the team's text still sits on the merged-away record), sent from the number the reaction came in on to the phone it came from, with status `dispatching`, `sent`, `delivered` or `dispatch_unknown`, dispatched before the reaction and created in the 30 days before it (the newest when several are equal); a cut or altered quote is never linked, and an unlinked text is not a reaction. [core + DB trigger]
- **R-REACT-3** A linked reaction is classified by its own words only (the template words and the emoji, with the tenant's keyword lists), never by its quote; every other inbound text is classified exactly as before D5 (property test). When the whole text would have read as an opt-out or a wrong number, the system audits `consent.reaction_quote_not_read` (reason: that reading), the notification and the thread say so plainly, and the thread offers the person-level do-not-contact in two taps to those who may edit the person. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (this narrows R-CONSENT-8, R-CONSENT-11 and R-CHAN-3 for quoted words that are provably the team's own). [core + api + web]
- **R-REACT-4** A reaction is a reply exactly like any inbound text (last-inbound times, the inbound sequence, the pause of every contact on the address, cancelled assistant messages and trigger runs, one handback, the feed card, a notification of the same kind and priority). Only the copy changes: the handback's client claim is a fact ("Sam loved “…”") citing the reaction text verbatim, and no copy says "your text". [api]
- **R-REACT-5** The link and the reaction (add or remove; a tapback name, a sticker or the emoji as received) are one `message_reactions` row, written only by the system inside the inbound transaction. A refused or failed write rolls back to a savepoint, is audited `consent.reaction_link_refused`, and the text is classified as before; a deadlock or serialization failure retries the whole ingestion. The row is never updated, is deleted only with either message, and is readable exactly by those who can read the reaction text; the raw text stays on the message, searchable and one tap away. [DB + api]
- **R-REACT-6** The thread shows each linked reaction as a line naming the reaction and the team's text, and the reaction in force on the reacted-to bubble (the latest add, cleared by a matching removal, replaced by a later add; emoji compared with only U+FE0F removed). An unlinked reaction-shaped text shows as an ordinary text. Between a Team Lead's merge and its follow-up, when the reacted-to text still sits on the merged-away record the viewer cannot read, the line still shows, quoting the person's own message (dropped only if it does not parse), with the not-an-opt-out line and the do-not-contact offer; after the follow-up the quote is the team's text again (backlog gaps, E555). [web + api]
- **R-REACT-7** The assistant reads a linked reaction as the client's reaction to the team's stored text, never as the client's words: the prompt prints only the reaction (as untrusted data) and the handle of the team's text (added to the bundle when older than the loaded messages), the reaction's evidence source holds only its own words, and draft categories (handback drafts, the approved reply, run drafts) ignore the quote. [core + api]

### Valuation and CMA (D9)
Owner decision D9: values and CMAs come from the team's own MLS sold data, built in-house, never a third-party AVM; an estimate, never an appraisal; behind the MLS licence pack and counsel review. The comps are the evidence. Lanes: V1-A (sold data and valuation gates, modules 23/24), V1-B (core/valuation, module 70), V1-C (api/cma and web/cma, modules 71/72), V2-A/V2-B (sharing; the in-house AVM provider). V2-B's parts: in-house home-value notes (cma/avm.ts InHouseAvm, automatedGate), seller-update value ranges (cma/sellerValue.ts), the compliance sweep's cancel of queued in-house notes, and the re-check at every approval of a home-value draft. Module 70 (core/valuation) owns R-CMA-2…5, module 24 with lane V1-A R-CMA-1, module 71 (api/cma) R-CMA-6…8, V2 R-CMA-9…10. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL / MLS** throughout.
- **R-CMA-1** (stored sale facts) MLS sale and property facts are stored from the feed as columns: close price and date, contract date, original list price, concessions, sale conditions, days on market; coordinates (integer microdegrees, with a generated cell), year built, lot size (sq ft), subdivision (placeholders stored as none), garage spaces and stories (tenths), private pool; the size field's source, a lease flag, InternetAutomatedValuationDisplayYN, and an address key built from the structured address with the state folded to its USPS code. A missing, unreadable or out-of-bound value is null and never blocks the record. A record the database still refuses is skipped, and its batch continues. The AVM flag is true only for an explicit yes. A lease is never a sale. Rows stored before these fields existed are filled by a backfill pass that never inserts, never alerts, writes no price history and never delays or masks the incremental feed. [core + api]
- **R-CMA-2** (what a comp is) A comparable is: a closed sale (status sold, close price > 0 and plausible: not below the minimum sale price or 20 % of its original list price unless the agent includes it, close date on or before the valuation date and within the look-back); not a lease; of the subject's property type and size measure; not the subject itself (not its listing, a mirror, its MLS numbers or its address key); counted once (R-MLS-5); of standard or unrecorded sale conditions, unless the agent includes it. The narrowest area tier is the same subdivision within the same postal code or 3 mi. Selection reads property, transaction, time and location facts only: strict input schemas (subject, sale and reference), no listing text or photos, no contact, demographic, school, crime or prediction data (R-PRED-4 lint, import-boundary test). Every comp states why it was chosen and its distance, or "distance not known". [core: types + runtime validator, lint]
- **R-CMA-3** (adjustments) Every adjustment is derived from the same run's local sales: a time trend from each comp's contract date (else its close date, noted), and robust (Theil–Sen) slopes over local sales for the other features. Each adjustment is shown with its rate, its support in sales (with and without, or above and below) and a sentence. A feature without enough support (at least 10 sales on each side and 30 pairs, and 3 distinct values for a non-binary feature), or with a wrong-signed rate for a feature that cannot lower value (size, baths, garage, pool, lot), is not adjusted and says so. Concessions only lower a price. No adjustment has a fixed percentage cap. [core]
- **R-CMA-4** (value and range) The value is the weighted median of the adjusted comps. The weights come from how little each comp needed adjusting, leaving out the size adjustment, and are shown per comp. Size is the one adjustment that moves with the subject's own size, and leaving it out keeps a larger subject from ever lowering the value (F12). With size included, about 1 run in 70 of the property test broke that. Size similarity still filters (±20 %) and ranks. The value lies within the lowest-to-highest adjusted range. Fewer than 3 comps ⇒ no value (INSUFFICIENT_COMPS). The evidence is the comps, the adjustments, the date and distance spans, the tier reads, and an out-of-sample back-test on recent local sales. No confidence, probability or score field exists (R-AST-13). [core + api snapshot]
- **R-CMA-5** (determinism) The same inputs give the same result in any order, in integer cents. Every stored result carries its method version, and changing any default changes the method version (a test pins `CMA_DEFAULTS` to `CMA_METHOD_VERSION`; a result made with a rule override carries `cma/1+custom-rule`). [core]
