- **R-CMA-6** (agent CMA and readers) An agent CMA needs a live MLS connection whose licence pack records valuation use (valuation.agent_cma); otherwise the API says why in words. A stale feed is shown as "MLS data as of …", not blocked. Preparing, editing and reading through the API need `cma.prepare`: Owner/Admin all, Team Lead team, Agent own and collaborated; ISA and TC have none. In the database, a stored CMA is readable only by its creator, by people who see the contact's detail (for a merged-away record, the detail of the record its merge chain ends at: since `0077_b11_review_documents_cma` the survivor's agent and Team Lead read and list its CMAs, batch 11 review ownership-data/F1), and by tenant-wide roles. A one-tap prepare reuses or supersedes only the caller's own draft. [api + RLS]
- **R-CMA-7** (client gates) Nothing derived from MLS sold data (value, range or comp) reaches a client unless all of these hold: its platform pack is cleared (platform:cma for client CMAs and seller-update value ranges, platform:avm for automated home-value notes; a note whose value is derived from MLS sold data needs platform:cma and platform:avm at every stage, a human-approved draft included); the connection is client-eligible (R-MLS-2/3); the pack's valuation section allows that use (client_cma, or automated_notes for notes and seller-update ranges (seller-update ranges also client_cma until V1 is answered)), is counsel-reviewed and has a valuation statement for the subject's state; every comp shown passes applyDisplayRules(client) with sold data allowed (a mirrored team sale through clientSoldView); no live MLS record of the subject withholds automated valuations under the pack's avm_flag_scope; no possible live listing of the subject is unconfirmed (automated outputs treat a near match as withheld). Every client rendering carries the R-AVM-1 sentence, the statement, the attribution and disclaimer, and "MLS data as of …". The gate is checked where the output is built and again in the transaction that queues it. The compliance sweep cancels queued shares, and the notes it can link, whenever readiness fails. Dispatch checks opt-outs only (D1). V2 shares only through R-CMA-9. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL / MLS.** [api + core]
- **R-CMA-8** (stored CMAs) A stored CMA keeps its subject facts with their sources, its result, its method version, its valuation date, the MLS data date and its disclosures. A draft is edited only by its creator or a tenant-wide role, and by nobody once its record was merged away (`CONTACT_MERGED`). A shared or superseded CMA never changes (DB guard), except foreign keys nulled by deletion. A person shares as themselves. A contact's CMAs are erased with the contact. [DB + api]
- **R-CMA-9** (V2: sharing)
  - **One tap.** Sharing is one human tap by the CMA's creator, after a preview that shows the exact report, the recipient's D1 decision and every blocker.
  - **Recipients.** The recipient is the CMA's contact, or a co_seller or spouse_partner relation the sharer sees and may message (comms.send), at one email point. A research CMA or a listing-subject CMA is not shared.
  - **The email.** It is a human one-to-one email, created in the sharer's own transaction (ARCHITECTURE §9 path 1) and sent by dispatchOutbound (D1, R-GATE-14). Its purpose follows the human classification; it is never forced to informational.
  - **That transaction refuses the share unless:** R-CMA-7 holds again; the stored content and the rendered report equal what the preview showed; every comp's live sold price, sold date, beds, baths and size equal the stored ones, and the stored MLS data date is within every used pack's freshness window; for a purpose other than a listing price or a buyer's offer, the SoP 11-1 items are recorded: intended users, interest, conflicts, defined value, limiting conditions, and inspection and date (VERIFY V5/V7); an inspection always has its date; the fair-housing guard passes on the agent's own text (hard ⇒ refused, soft ⇒ "Check this").
  - **Contents.** The report carries the range (never a point value), the estimate sentence, every used connection's statement, attribution and disclaimer, the MLS data date, and the comps the engine used as each one renders for clients now. It never carries list prices, not-used or context listings, the subject's sale history, weights, rates or the back-test.
  - **Freeze.** The CMA freezes with its message in one UPDATE.
  - **Sweep.** The sweep cancels a queued share (including one held for a re-tap) whenever readiness fails. The CMA stays shared and the view says why.
  - **PDF.** The PDF is served only for a shared CMA, re-checked at download (attachment, nosniff, no-store, audited).
  - **Statements.** No statement, attribution or disclaimer is rendered with a substituted or compatibility-folded character (curly quotes, dashes and NBSP excepted). A statement is never smaller than 12 pt or the state's minimum. A plain-text email does not carry a minimum above 12 pt.
  - **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** [api + web]
- **R-CMA-10** (V2: in-house values) The in-house AvmProvider values only from the tenant's own MLS rows, from connections whose pack records the use, in its own read-only system transaction (no contact lock, no network call). A data reason (no MLS facts, an unsupported subject, no R-CMA-4 value) is an unavailable estimate; a configuration or sync failure records nothing. It dates the estimate by the feed's last sync (tenant-local), names its source "recent MLS sales", never a vendor, and stores `inhouse:{method}:{connections}:{inputs sha256}`; a malformed ref records nothing; an in-house estimate without a valid ref is never displayable. displayAllowed needs platform:cma and platform:avm cleared; every connection read passing valuationEligible for automated_notes (fresh feed included) with a reviewed display pack, attribution, disclaimer and a statement for the state; no live MLS record or live team listing at the subject's key; no live near match; every pack text rendering unchanged. avm.refresh requests nothing until both packs are cleared and a live connection records agent_cma and automated_notes with a reviewed valuation section. The gate is re-checked where the note is queued (a failure is a skip, never a draft) and at every approval of a draft carrying one, whose body must keep every required line; the sweep cancels queued in-house notes once a pack or a read connection fails; a not-displayable estimate's fact shows no number; "MLS data as of" names the estimate's own date. The seller update's 'value' section follows R-CMA-7 with automated_notes (and client_cma until V1 is answered), each comp through clientView or clientSoldView as a closed sale with its sold price and brokerage; valuation date the period's last local day; configuration gates read before any sold data; a data reason goes to the seller, a configuration reason to the agent only; email only, removable by the listing agent, re-checked before any message is queued. [api + core]

### Deploy and the database role model

Code rules of the release step, the preflight CLI and the API and worker start-up (pilot deploy track; no migration, no table change; docs/DEPLOY.md §3, §12).

- **R-DEPLOY-1** The API and the worker connect only as app_user, and any failure of these checks refuses start [api start-up + release + db test]:
  - current_user = session_user = app_user;
  - NOSUPERUSER NOBYPASSRLS NOCREATEROLE NOCREATEDB NOREPLICATION;
  - a member of no role; owns no object outside temporary namespaces; holds CREATE on no schema;
  - every public table has RLS enabled and forced (0001's self-check predicate);
  - zero tenants visible without context.
- **R-DEPLOY-2** Migrations run only as a role that bypasses RLS (superuser or BYPASSRLS). The runner refuses before any pending file otherwise, and before creating its bookkeeping schema on a fresh database. The release step and the preflight also prove the bypass by behaviour, with a FORCE-RLS update probe. [db]
- **R-DEPLOY-3** The release step changes app_user only by LOGIN, its password, and the wrong attributes the owner may change. CREATEDB, BYPASSRLS and REPLICATION count only if the owner holds them; SUPERUSER only if the owner is superuser. It refuses an attribute it may not change, naming it, and asserts all seven attributes afterwards. [db]
- **R-DEPLOY-4** With a non-superuser owner [db]:
  - the definer roles hold CREATE on a schema only inside the release step's window around migrate(), failure included; a later run closes a window left open first;
  - afterwards they hold no direct CREATE grant anywhere;
  - every SECURITY DEFINER function outside the system schemas and extensions is owned by app_definer or app_retention;
  - both definer roles stay NOLOGIN NOSUPERUSER BYPASSRLS, checked on every release.
- **R-DEPLOY-5** In production, every database connection uses TLS with chain and host-name verification from DATABASE_SSL_*. That covers the api, worker, release, migrate, counsel-review, KEK rotation and seed. URLs carry no query parameters, and `PG*` environment variables are refused. `disable` is allowed only with ALLOW_FAKE_PROVIDERS=true (staging). A TLS failure is a named start-up refusal. [api config + db]
- **R-DEPLOY-6** On the app VM, owner credentials exist only for the release window, in a separate root-only file that only the release profile reads. api, worker, web and edge never reference it. [compose test]
- **R-DEPLOY-7** The repository holds secret references, never values. The renderer [test]:
  - writes 0600 files with a strict value charset;
  - never prints a value;
  - keeps owner and libpq names out of the app file;
  - keeps the app-VM templates' keys equal to env.production.example's.

### Extensions of Phase 1 families
- **R-AST-19** The new assistant kinds have their autonomy fixed by CHECK: `marketing_draft`, `ad_reallocation` (money), `review_reply_draft` (novel_message) and `contract_extraction` (contract) always need approval; `call_review` and `weekly_plan` are internal. [DB]
- **R-AST-20** No new autonomous category is added; new automated outputs (trigger alerts, milestone updates, home values, review requests) run only as steps of an approved playbook in an existing autonomous category. [core + DB]
- **R-AST-21** A decision on an assistant action (approve, reject) is made by a person, as themselves: the database records the decider's role from memberships at that moment, never lets the system or another person decide in someone's name, never rewrites a decision (reverting to `proposed` clears it) and never executes a requires-approval action that was not approved; an `ad_reallocation` is decided by an Owner or Admin. [DB, smoke]
- **R-ACC-6** `milestone_at_risk` is the fifth exception definition (subject `deal_date`, key `milestone:{deal_date}:{due_version}`, owner the deal owner): a confirmed date with `completed_at` NULL that is overdue (`due_at < now`), or due within 48 h (`due_at - now ≤ 48 h`) with no `responsible` party; the evidence names which predicate holds. [core + job]
- **R-OWN-27** Every Phase 2/3 timer stores its instant when set and carries a fence; each is woken by the owning module's per-tenant reconcile handler (`JobHandler.every`, one line in `worker/handlers/index.ts`) rather than a new `app.due_work` branch: MLS and feed sync from `integration_connections.next_sync_at` (module 24, every 60 s), trigger firings from `scheduled_for` (26, 60 s), home values and anniversaries (26, daily), seller updates from `listings.seller_update_next_due_at` (28, 5 min), milestone reminders from `deal_dates.reminder_at` plus the `due_at` repair (31, 5 min), weekly plans (33, hourly, per user and local Monday), daily fixes (36, hourly, per user and local date), prediction refresh (50, nightly per tenant and date), homeowner refresh (78, nightly per tenant and local date, `homeowner_profiles.next_refresh_at`; D10), ad reconcile (52, hourly per campaign and hour), Gmail sync from `user_mail_links.next_sync_at`, plus revoked links that still have copies (68, every `platform:gmail` `poll_interval_seconds`, 120 s by default; D2). The voice takeover is the telephony dial timeout decided at call start; calendar tokens refresh inside the push; `portal_grants.expires_at` is compared with `now()` in the policy. Settings changes move only deadlines set afterwards. [core + db]
- **R-DATA-6** Retention for Phase 2/3 data (all **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**, configurable within ranges): DM/chat bodies as messages; contactless chat sessions 30 d; MLS rows hidden per licence; **D9 (F9), what the code does:** MLS rows, closed sales included, are kept: no MLS purge exists (`mls/sweep.ts` only marks stale feeds, cancels alerts and links mirrors), so the old "purged 180 d after off-market" (which would have deleted every comp older than 6 months) is not built either. The CMA reads sold rows a fixed 30 months back from its valuation date (`cma/service.ts`: CMA_DEFAULTS' 6-month back-test window plus the 24-month extended and rate-pool reach), and the engine compares sales within 12 months, extended to 24 (core/valuation CMA_DEFAULTS, **VERIFY**). A stored CMA copies sold facts into `cma_reports.subject` and `.result` (the comps' addresses, MLS numbers, close prices and dates, the subject's sale history), and goes only with its contact (erasure, R-DATA-7). **Gap (batch 10 review ownership-data/F6, not built; VERIFY WITH COUNSEL (C13) / VERIFY WITH MLS (V1)):** (a) the licence pack's valuation section has no retention or look-back limit (`ValuationLicenceSchema`: agent_cma, client_cma, automated_notes, avm_flag_scope, statements, reviewed_by_counsel_at), so nothing caps the 30-month read, the engine's look-back or the stored sold rows by the licence, and no result states a licence limit; (b) CMA reports have no retention default (C13; 0061, module 71 and `platform:cma` are dark) and nothing removes or redacts them on MLS disconnect or licence end; (c) a later purge of `listings` rows would leave the sold facts copied into stored CMAs. Building any of these is a later change (a licence key, a capped `since`, a retention definer); AVM values 13 months or the licence term; property records per licence (≤ 12 months); predictions latest + 90 d; OAuth tokens 30 d after revoke; marketing pieces life of listing + 3 years; documents 7 years after close (3–10; two recorded gaps: nothing is purged after retention ends, and the 7-year default is a code constant, `DOCUMENT_RETENTION_YEARS`, so a tenant setting needs a reviewed schema change; R-DOC-10); voice recordings as recordings (730 d); voice tool calls (no PII by construction) and ad ledgers kept; call scores with their recording. `home_valuations` are purged by the system-only, tenant-filtered retention definer `app.purge_home_valuations()` (owner `app_retention`, no argument, database clock), which every `avm.refresh` pass calls first in its own system transaction, whether or not a vendor is configured: a valuation recorded (`valued_at`) more than 13 months ago is deleted (**VERIFY WITH COUNSEL C13, C6**; V13: whether 13 months fits D9's in-house valuation built from MLS data under the MLS licence term; owner question Q7: fixed for now) unless a trigger run of the tenant that is still `scheduled`, or `converted_to_draft` with its draft still `proposed` or `approved`, cites it (`trigger_ref.ref.valuationId`); such a row goes on the first pass after that run or draft settles. In the same call the deleted rows' citations keep no copy of the estimate (batch 11 review ownership-data/F3, migration `0076_b11_review_backlog`): their `evidence_refs` lose the snippet and are marked `source_available = false` (the evidence views and web `Evidence.tsx` then read "Source removed under retention policy", V10), and each trigger run's stored evidence drops the snippet and records `sourceAvailable: false` (the trigger-runs view says the source was removed). What the purge does not touch: a draft's own text (its body, its evidence line and its claim sentences, which quote the estimate as written) and a message already sent, which are the record of what was proposed or sent and follow the message retention above (owner question). app_user still has no DELETE; erasure still cascades; a deleted address leaves its valuations with `contact_address_id` NULL until they age out (backlog gaps, E553, E554). Property records and homeowner profiles (D10): at most 12 months after the last answered check, purged nightly by the system, and purged on lost recognition (as well as do-not-contact, merge, closing or deletion of the contact; R-HOME-9), cascading on erasure and address deletion (**VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**). D2: DM bodies n/a (no DMs); synced Gmail copies kept while the link lives and deleted within one sync interval after it is revoked; Gmail refresh tokens deleted at revoke, the revoked link and its exclusions purged 30 d after revoke. [DB + retention]
- **R-DATA-7** Erasure cascades to every new contact-scoped table (portal accounts and grants, property records, predictions, call scores, contact documents, linked chat sessions, D2: synced Gmail copies `mail_messages`), shreds the contact's document objects, keeps HMAC opt-outs and ledgers, and still refuses contacts on a deal or with a document on hold or inside its retention period. The preview (`GET /contacts/:id/erasure`) lists those documents across the merge tree (ids, holds, retention dates, and for the Owner/Admin page their kind, name, version, upload date and record) and is not erasable while one exists; the refusal names them by id, hold and date (batch 11 review). **Listing documents stay:** a seller's listing role goes with the erasure, but the documents of that listing (a listing agreement names the seller) are the listing's records and are not erased; the preview and the erasure page say so (**VERIFY WITH COUNSEL**: whether a listing agreement is a transaction record to keep, or must go with the person). **A merged-away record** can still be erased alone (it takes only what stayed on it, such as its documents); its preview names the record it was merged into, where its moved work lives, and the page points there for a person erasure (owner question). What stayed includes the recordings and transcripts of its calls: a merge moves the calls to the survivor but no merge path moves a recording (`recordings.contact_id` keeps the merged-away record), so erasing the record deletes them and the calls stay on the survivor without a recording. Its documents and CMA reports stay on it too (the survivor's viewers read them, 0075/0077) and go with it. The preview counts all three (`erasedWith`: call recordings not yet shredded, documents, CMA reports, over the records erased) and the page says them by count before the tap (batch 11 review ownership-data/F2). Documents never move on a merge; the survivor's viewers read a merged-away record's contact documents (0075). **AI ISA (batch 12):** the person's assignments and their controls, turns, shared facts, slot offers, handoffs and person blocks go with the erasure and the retention purge (the insert-only guards let only that cascade through); `ai_isa_stop_signals` stay as an opt-out ledger (AI_ISA.md §8.3). [DB, smoke]

### Telephony safeguards (lane telephony-safeguards; fakes only, nothing reaches Twilio)
- **R-TWL-1** Kill switch: `TELEPHONY_LIVE_REQUESTS` (default `false` in every environment) gates every outbound Twilio REST request in the adapter, which refuses it before making it with the provider error `TELEPHONY_LIVE_OFF` (sends, calls, number purchase and release, message lookups, registration reads, media and recording fetches and deletes). dispatch and click-to-call refuse a text or call in TX1, before the daily-cap unit and the touch, as a recorded gate deny `TELEPHONY_LIVE_OFF` ("Live texting and calling are off on this deployment."; b9 review ownership-data/F4: the adapter reports the switch, so a refused send is never a touch); the adapter still refuses anything else that reaches it; number purchase answers with the same sentence; it is never an opt-out, never retried and never reconciled. Inbound webhooks keep working. [api]
- **R-TWL-2** A per-tenant daily cap (`tenant_settings.telephony_daily_cap`, default 200, CHECK) on outbound texts and calls through the real adapter, enforced inside the send-time transaction before the provider call by one atomic claim on `telephony_daily_usage` (tenant-local day; counts only grow). Over the cap: a blocked send with the gate reason `DAILY_SEND_CAP_REACHED` ("Today's sending limit for this team is reached."). The platform's STOP confirmation and HELP reply are exempt. A client's sign-in link text (`portal_login_link`) claims a unit only while the day's `portal_link_sends` is also under `PORTAL_LINK_CAP_PERCENT` (owner default 10) % of the cap, rounded down, in the same statement (b14 review R1, migration 0090); over that share it is blocked `PORTAL_LINK_DAILY_CAP_REACHED` ("Today's limit on sign-in link texts for this team is reached.") and claims nothing. [api + DB]
- **R-TWL-3** The telephony webhook paths are a stable public contract (DEPLOY §5.2): each verifies `X-Twilio-Signature` over `PUBLIC_BASE_URL` + the path (never the Host header) and is idempotent on its MessageSid / CallSid / RecordingSid; a documented path that disappears from the route table fails a test. [api]
- **R-TWL-4** A status callback without `From` is accepted: the tenant is the one the signature guard resolved, else one unrouted `webhook_events` row with a `WEBHOOK_UNROUTED` log and a 200; `MessageSid` stays required. Status map: `canceled` → failed (`PROVIDER_CANCELED`), `scheduled` → ignored, `read` → delivered. [api]
- **R-TWL-5** No code path updates an existing number at Twilio: nothing POSTs to `IncomingPhoneNumbers/{sid}` and nothing writes `SmsUrl`, `VoiceUrl`, `StatusCallback` or an application SID, except number purchase (which R-TWL-1 blocks while off). [api, architecture test]
- **R-TWL-6** Request logs never carry `X-Twilio-Signature` (one exported redaction list, request and response headers). [api]
- **R-TWL-7** `OptOutType=STOP` from Twilio, when our own classification is not stop, opt-out language or wrong number, classifies the message as `stop` (matched `OPTOUTTYPE_STOP`) with the usual STOP effects and no platform reply (the provider sends its own). `OptOutType` START or HELP, and a body of YES, change nothing; YES never lifts an opt-out. [api]
- **R-TWL-8** Error 21610 on an agent alert text records the alert phone's SMS revoke exactly as the agent's own STOP does, marks the delivery suppressed `PROVIDER_OPTED_OUT`, audits it and notifies Admins; the agent's own START from that phone restores alerts. (Contact sends: R-CONSENT-12.) [api]
- **R-TWL-9** Error 30032 (toll-free not verified) or 30034 (unregistered A2P 10DLC sender; both VERIFY), from the submit or a status callback: the text fails with `SENDER_NOT_VERIFIED` ("Not delivered: the team's number isn't verified for texting. The Owner has been told."), never an opt-out, never retried or reconciled; in the same transaction THAT NUMBER is marked not ready to text (`phone_numbers.sender_not_verified_at` / `_detail`; never its registration, which other team numbers share: b9 review sends/F1), so the sender check blocks texts from it while calls keep working and every other number keeps texting; audited; one Owner/Admin notice per number per day naming that number and saying other numbers are not affected; the Numbers page shows "Texting paused for this number". "Check registration status" (a provider read, blocked while R-TWL-1 is off) clears it when the carrier reports the campaign approved; attaching a different registration clears it too (the same one again never does). A system keyword reply (STOP confirmation, HELP reply) never makes a tracking number the conversation's sticky sender. [api]
- **R-TWL-10** A keyword to a toll-free receiving number: the platform sends no STOP confirmation or HELP reply of its own (`optOutConfirmationSender` 'provider' for that message; the carrier answers). Local numbers are unchanged. VERIFY in the pilot test step. [api]
- **R-TWL-11** Text carried in MMS `text/plain` parts (`message_text_parts`, job `comms.mms_text` enqueued in the inbound message's own transaction): each part is read by SID through the adapter (blocked while R-TWL-1 is off), at most 16 KiB, strict UTF-8 with the BOM stripped (a NUL or invalid UTF-8 is unreadable); in one transaction (address lock, then contact locks) the part is marked read, classified, and every revoking kind the Body did not already produce (stop, opt-out language, wrong number) is applied. A part never lifts an opt-out and never gets its own reply; a STOP found only in a part is confirmed by the platform once per message. A keyword reply (this confirmation, and the Body's STOP confirmation or HELP reply) goes from the texted number when it can text, else from the team main number when that can (it still reaches only the phone that texted the keyword), else it is recorded blocked with its reason (b9 review sends/F3). An unreadable, oversized or refused part is a skipped row plus one notice to the handler; a fetch the kill switch refused is recorded as `LIVE_REQUESTS_OFF` (inbound media too) and said as the switch, never the carrier ("it wasn't downloaded: live texting and calling are off on this deployment"). The thread shows read parts as plain text and pending ones as "Part of this text hasn't been read yet."; parts never reach an assistant prompt. [api + DB]

AI ISA (D12 PROPOSED; docs/AI_ISA.md §12; migration `0080_ai_isa.sql`, lane number 0190). These rules apply only while the owner's decision D12 is in force and only to AI ISA messages; every other path keeps today's rules. VERIFY WITH COUNSEL where marked.

- **R-ISA-1** Authorization chain. All §3.1 links, default off; checked at start, ingestion, TX-A, TX-B, TX1 and the guard. One link-failure table (hold, pause or end) applied identically everywhere. [DB + core + api]
- **R-ISA-2** Exclusivity. One live assignment per contact, and none on a shared address (any point, any kind, removed included unless wrong_number) or for an active client, checked continuously. A start stops the enrollment and supersedes drafts and handbacks. While care is live: no enrollment, triggers become events, stage automation skips. [DB + api]
- **R-ISA-3** The reply rule under care. Step 6 unchanged. Only a bound-point, DKIM-aligned, media-free SMS or email from the client is a turn. Every other inbound runs R-AST-9 and pauses care. Takeover on a one-to-one human create or schedule, on draft or handback approval, or on a dial. Bulk and marketing sends never take over. Fail closed per R-ISA-1. [api + DB]
- **R-ISA-4** Concurrency. The global lock order. No provider call under locks 2–4. One turn at a time. Debounce through retryAt. TX-B guarded by attempt. The guard checks the pinned seq, the epoch and the body hash. The single job-lease recovery path. [api + DB]
- **R-ISA-5** Model output. A closed, strict action set. The model proposes and core decides. No free-text notes. The summary is screened and handoff-only. Decisions stored as codes and handles only. [core + api]
- **R-ISA-6** Escalations. The §4.4 taxonomy, with terminal versus hold_topic and the precedence. Deterministic and negation-aware. Statements are qualification; requests escalate. Fixed lines; the safety line is never deferred. [core]
- **R-ISA-7** Content. Model text is conversation only (the §7.3 guard, including market and comparative claims). A facts message has no model text. The disclosure and sign-off are platform constants containing "AI". Never claims to be human. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** [core]
- **R-ISA-8** Send checks. D1 first and unchanged. Purpose is always marketing. Plus R-ISA-9/10/11/25/28/31 in core, dispatch and the guard; the guard re-derives the kind, tier and zones. The reason classes. Non-interference for other categories. [core + api + DB]
- **R-ISA-9** Consent evidence. The reply tier from the client's own bound inbound in the window. The initiated and facts tier from the person's own disclosure: named tenant in the body, scope stated, after D12, not imported, not revoked, plus a fresh DNC result for SMS. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** [core + DB]
- **R-ISA-10** Quiet hours. Explicit only when a person set it; otherwise the conservative union; the window intersection; replies included; initiated days and holidays; no overlap hands off; the guard recomputes the minimum set; the offset agreement check. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** [core + DB]
- **R-ISA-11** Caps. 3 per 24 h across AI messages, with AI yielding to the person's total automated and bulk count. Initiated limits. A 120 s gap. One open message. Address-keyed sorted locks. The counted statuses. The daily upsert. Slow, dormant and delivery_problem. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** [DB + core]
  - *Race statement (ISA-2b).* One person's AI messages never pass the guard on stale counts: all go to the root contact (the guard requires `aa.contact_id = assignment.contact_id`, root only), one proposed AI action per contact (`assistant_actions_ai_isa_open_uq`), TX-B queues only under the turn locks, and dispatch TX1 holds `lock_contact` to commit. So a newer message either follows the older one's committed TX1 (whose row its guard counts) or supersedes the older one first, which the guard then refuses (ASSISTANT_ACTION_WITHDRAWN); the per-HMAC send locks serialize anyway. The nearest real race, the AI against another automated or bulk send on ONE record, serializes on `lock_contact`: whichever TX1 commits first is counted by the other, and the AI defers ISA_CAP_REACHED; non-AI sends never meet AI caps (D1 only). Residual: a bulk send to ANOTHER record of the same person (a different `lock_contact`) can race the AI's TX1, so the person may get at most one message over the automated yield threshold, never over the AI's own 24 h cap. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (the residual-risk sentence: whether one message over the automated yield threshold is acceptable; batch 13 audit ISA2B-12).
- **R-ISA-12** Readiness. B1–B3 and S1–S3 from usable client own-text claims, with `qualValueQuoted`. It waits for a live offer. Recorded with rule, version and claim ids. A disqualifier pauses for a check. [core]
- **R-ISA-13** Appointment boundary. A live calendar read; one explicitly agreed, offered slot from client own text; a confirmation that can be sent ahead of notice; booking in its own job with `requireLiveCalendar` and `expectLinkId`; the lock trigger and assistant overlap refusal; never an auto-booked alternative; a templated confirmation in the post-handoff set. [core + api + DB]
  - *ISA-2b notes.* One open appointment per person: TX-O refuses an offer (APPOINTMENT_ALREADY_SET, Q3) and the book job re-reads under the organizer's booking lock; across two organizers the residual is that two different agents' AI bookings for one person could both pass (each holds only its own organizer's lock); **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (the cross-organizer residual; batch 13 audit ISA2B-12). confirmationInTime counts the 24 h AI and automated caps and the minimum gap (the API's nextSendAt; a core follow-up), and the send window is read at the instant the caps clear and the gap passes, not at `now` (batch 13 audit ISA2B-02: caps clearing at 20:30 wait for 08:00). An acknowledgement ("ok", "great", "perfect", a tapback) is never agreement: one clarification (Q10).
- **R-ISA-14** Handoff. Exactly one per assignment; it ends care; a frozen package; facts sent only; open offers closed; one card per event; the post-handoff closed set. [api + DB]
  - *ISA-2b as built (cut 2; batch 13 audit ISA2B-04).* "Open offers closed" holds for the offer ROW (superseded with the end, and the package's `openOffer` names it), not for a client line: the brief's cut 2 (core's `open_offer_closed` holding line added to the end's line when core ends care in a live turn with a delivered offer) was taken and is not built. No line tells the client the offer is closed; the agent's card shows the open offer. Building it needs the API to add the line only for ends core takes in a live turn, never after a human control, pause, revoke, stop, block or tenant off (post-handoff messages skip the opt-in check, 0190:1042).
- **R-ISA-15** Controls. Pause, resume, take over and end, at lead, agent, version, tenant and pack level. Controls ledger. Cancels in a system transaction. Epoch on resume. 10 s undo. Pause cards. 72 h expiry. The system never clears a pause. Immutable bindings. [DB + api]
  - *ISA-2b notes.* After a take over the handoff's recipient (card and deadline) is the person who took over, from the latest `take_over` control row, while the lead is still with the assignment's owner (batch 13; otherwise b12 review F1's routing); the owner is notified (`ai_isa_ended`); an End keeps the owner with `endedByUserId`. Resume is refused while an offer is being booked (AI_ISA_BOOKING_IN_PROGRESS).
- **R-ISA-16** Ownership. Start needs the first touch. Owner change, merge or deactivation ends care in the same transaction (stop signal). A same-owner reinquiry pauses. Inactivity warning hands off. The AI never changes ownership. [DB + api]
- **R-ISA-17** Buyer grounding. clientView and row freshness; the asking price labelled; ≤ 3 per message; never repeated; no keywords; saved searches proposed only, with AI-origin alerts suppressed while care is live and re-approved after the handoff. [api + core]
  - *ISA-2b notes.* Facts channel plan: the inbound's channel if the initiated tier passes and (SMS) the body fits 3 segments; else email; else SMS with whole lines dropped; never the email pointer. Leases (and rows not yet lease-checked: normalize version < 2), off-market rows and team listings without a live, displayed MLS mirror are never shared. AI-sent homes are marked alerted on the AI-origin search at the handoff.
- **R-ISA-18** Seller grounding. D9 selection and V1-C `clientReadiness` only. `cma.prepare` on behalf of the owner. An agent-confirmed subject. The complete comp set or nothing, by email only. platform:cma plus platform:ai_isa_seller_data plus `valuation.ai_isa_comps`. Ceilings. Labels (sold vs asking, closed vs pending vs active, comparable vs nearby, with the reason whitelist). No hidden counts. No value in v1. Pricing goes to the human. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL / MLS.** [api + core]
  - **As built (ISA-3):** comps come only from `compsForSubjectInTx` (api/cma, read-only, one caller: `isa/seller/comps.ts`), run by the system via `assistant` (`ai_isa.seller_facts`) on behalf of the lead's owner holding `cma.prepare` (`canAsRole`; an owner demoted to isa or suspended gets none: CMA_SCOPE_LOST), with `clientReadiness(use 'ai_isa')` at build (TX-A, H1), at queue (TX-B, H2) and in dispatch TX1 (H3). That use reads platform:cma and platform:ai_isa_seller_data (never platform:avm), the licence's `ai_isa_comps` and V1-A's `'ai_isa_comps'` gate; SELLER_WITHHELD holds under either flag scope and near matches are never dismissable. The feed's age (FEED_STALE), not a sold comp's row age, decides freshness. Client text: V1-B's own area sentences (each asserted to be among the engine's), each comp's tier sentence (distance only with a shown address), the earliest sync of the comps' feeds, the state statements printable at ≤ 12 pt (V2-A; a fail-closed stub until then), the pack's `client_lines` verbatim; the server-text ban screens ISA and engine lines only. The subject is shown only through the client rendering; a changed record or default address unconfirms it (SUBJECT_CHANGED). Ceilings: tiers subdivision/within_1mi/postal_code/within_3mi, a 12-month look-back, both filters, ≥ 3 comps, no agent label. A pricing hold suspends comps until the owner's own new or re-run CMA, a shared CMA, or resume-comps (E502).
- **R-ISA-19** Market changes. Only uncapped, displayable counts by status, with thresholds and version checks. No sold-derived statistics in v1. No forecasts. [core + api]
  - **As built (ISA-3, `isa/seller/counts.ts`):** counts are computed for the agent only ("Not sent to clients yet") and never queued (`COUNT_SHARE_ENABLED = false`) until owner question 2 is resolved and the MLS confirms the rows are complete; feed-fresh, not row-fresh (an incremental feed moves `last_seen_in_feed_at` only on change).
- **R-ISA-20** Prompt isolation. One contact; nonce fences; handles with structured labels only; client own text; no notes, transcripts, remarks, prices or synced mail; tool-less; the mail-sync barrier; the hostile suite. [core + api]
  - **As built (ISA-3, H8):** a sent message that carried facts enters the prompt as a fixed placeholder (`[recent sales emailed]` for sold facts, `[listing details sent]` otherwise), never its body. No MLS price or sold fact enters the prompt; the seller bundle gives the model handles with structured labels only (H1).
- **R-ISA-21** Audit and evidence. Every turn with its versions; every control and system action audited; facts recorded at send; the frozen package. [api + DB]
- **R-ISA-22** Visibility. The list and panel show what it may do, why, its last decision, held topics, deadlines and inactivity dates. No confidence fields. [api + web]
- **R-ISA-23** Always-human topics. Money, offers, contracts, negotiation, commission, legal and lending are never AI content. The DB CHECK ties the mandate to the AI ISA categories. [DB + core]
- **R-ISA-24** Channels. SMS and email only. No calls, MMS or chat. Never touches, SLA or scoreboard. [core + DB]
- **R-ISA-25** Live safeguards. AI_ISA_LIVE_SENDS and TELEPHONY_LIVE_REQUESTS default false. The switches and caps hold. No self-retry. [api]
- **R-ISA-26** Represented elsewhere. A listing by another brokerage at the subject, or a confirmed other agent, ends care. A near match pauses. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** [api]
  - **As built (ISA-3):** checked at start (H4: `AI_ISA_LISTED_ELSEWHERE` or `AI_ISA_ACTIVE_CLIENT` for a seller or both-side start) and as link 10 "Seller home" of the chain (H5, `isa/seller/gates.ts subjectLinkInTx`): a live team listing or mirror at the subject's address key ends care `active_client`; another live MLS row at the key ends it `listed_elsewhere`; an undismissed live near match pauses it `possible_listing`. The owner's "different home" (`near_match_different`) plus the owner's resume lifts the pause; comps stay blocked while the near match is live (owner question 1). E538.

**`ai_isa_seller_watch` (ISA-3, migration `0084_ai_isa_seller`, lane number 0191).** One row per seller- or both-side assignment (PK `(tenant_id, assignment_id)`, cascades with it): the confirmed subject (`contact_address_id` → `contact_addresses (tenant_id, contact_id, id)` ON DELETE SET NULL (contact_address_id), `subject_address_key`, `_connection_id`, `_record_listing_id`, `_record_date`, `subject_facts` {type, beds, baths_x10, sqft}, `subject_confirmed_by_user_id` and `_at`, `subject_disputed_at`, `subject_cleared_reason`), `near_match_dismissed`, the competition `last_snapshot` (asking prices only) and `snapshot_versions` (the display-rules version per feed and the rule version), `last_comps_check` (codes, the comp-set hash and when; CHECK codes only), `comps_suspended_at` and `comps_resumed_*`, `last_run_at`. RLS: FORCE, the tenant and support policies, the support statement trigger; SELECT the contact-detail audience (staff) or tenant-wide, or the detail audience of the record's root survivor (batch 13 merge, 0081's db-isa-merge-2 rule, E526); INSERT the system or the assignment's owner as themselves; UPDATE the system or the contact-detail audience (the root survivor's too), narrowed by the invoker guard `ai_isa_seller_watch_guard` (only the live assignment's owner, as a user, confirms the subject at the database clock, dismisses a near match or resumes comps; the system never confirms and resumes only after a CMA; any other user only clears the subject or marks it disputed; `contact_address_id` becoming NULL clears it for anyone, with or without a tenant context, so the retention purge's foreign key action never trips the CHECK; R-DATA-7); no DELETE: erasure removes it with its assignment. `ai_isa_subject_address_cleared` (AFTER UPDATE of the address columns, as the editor) clears a confirmation; `ai_isa_seller_fact_email_only` refuses a `sold_comp_set` or `nearby_sale` fact on a non-email message. No SECURITY DEFINER.
- **R-ISA-27** Client own text. Quotes for qualification, readiness, booking and end come only from client own text on a bound point. Quoted email history is stripped. QUOTE_IS_TEAM_TEXT. [core]
- **R-ISA-28** Bound points. Points are bound at start and immutable. A turn only from a bound point (with an aligned DKIM pass for email). Outbound only to a bound point. A removed bound point ends care. [api + DB]
- **R-ISA-29** Answered-inbound backstop. An inbound under care is answered, scheduled for reply, handed off or answered by a human; otherwise the card is written at 10 minutes, or at once on any transition out of live. [api]
- **R-ISA-30** Handoff deadline. Per-class deadlines in business time, cleared only by a human touch; a person closing the lead also ends it, and so does the owner's explicit Continue AI care or policy re-attestation for the continued-from assignment's own handoff (b12 recheck R16, R23; owner question). "Got it" and a restart of AI care do not. Escalation to TLs, then Owner/Admin. Ownerless routing. [api + core]
- **R-ISA-31** Stop signals and blocks. Any opt-out, suppression or do-not-contact from any writer ends care in the same transaction. No email fallback after one. A person block, with strict lift conditions. "Not an opt-out" never lifts it. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** [DB + api]
- **R-ISA-32** Loosening controls. Owner-only switch-on and cap raise (re-auth plus attestation, DB guard); opt-in re-auth; recomputed attestation hashes. [DB + api]

### Module 55 (web/phase3-staff)

The Phase 3 staff screens: AI call answering settings and its handback card, the Google Ads page and its Approvals card, documents and the contract-date review, the likely-to-move list, Google Business Profile reviews and coaching trends. Every read is the person's own RLS read; module 55 adds two SELECT-only routes (`GET /likely`, `GET /documents/extractions`) and no write, migration, pack write or provider.

- **R-STAFF-1** Honest dark states. Each module 55 screen takes its state and its sentences only from its API. While a pack is uncleared or an adapter is unconfigured, the screen shows the server's reason in words, without a raw pack key or code, and shows no control for the gated action. The API still refuses. No code, fixture or env default marks a feature as cleared (D7, D11). [web]
- **R-STAFF-2** One decision path per kind. A contract extraction is decided only claim by claim on its document's date review; Approvals shows only a pointer. An ad shift is decided only through /ads/proposals/:id/approve|reject, and a review reply only through /gbp/reviews/:id/reply|discard. No generic /assistant route is used for any of them, including the generic approve, which only acknowledges. [web + test]
- **R-STAFF-3** No confidence, probability, score, points, percentage, grade or rank appears in any module 55 view type or text. Bands and directions are words. Every figure carries its denominator: back-tests "M of N flagged transacted within W days", trends "met X of Y scored calls", stars "n of 5", and ads totals against the cap or ceiling. A withheld count says it is withheld, never 0. Lists are never ordered by hidden points. [web snapshot + api]
- **R-STAFF-4** Money and attestations. Cap and voice attestations are shown and sent verbatim from the API (attestationText, details.expected), with re-auth through the shell. One approval is one tap through useAction and is never retried automatically. An unknown outcome is shown as waiting and is never sent again. A refusal (stale, superseded, cap changed, owner required) is shown in words, with the fresh state. [web]
- **R-STAFF-5** Untrusted text. Review text and reviewer, document and campaign names render as React text only. Downloads are same-origin `download` links, never rendered inline. Hrefs built from data go through safeUrl. [web]
- **R-STAFF-6** Role-gated fetches and controls. No Owner/Admin-only route (ads, voice settings, review replies, team trends without the grant) is requested for a role that lacks it. Upload controls show only to roles that hold the scope's upload action. A page a role cannot open shows "Not available" and never mounts. [web]
- **R-STAFF-7** The likely list shows only the viewer's own open likely-to-move cards, under the feed's filters (deleted contacts, R-PRED-5, R-PRED-6), with the card's own reasons and never a prediction's points. Its only action is opening the contact (R-PRED-4). [api + web]
- **R-STAFF-8** Upload pre-check. Any file other than a PDF, JPEG or PNG, an empty file, or a file over 10,000,000 bytes is refused in words before any request. The form carries only the fields the API reads. The server decides again (R-DOC-5). [web]
- **R-STAFF-9** The voice handback card shows the stored call fields and ledger codes in words. It never shows a raw stored value, a transcript or an in-call quote. The one-tap Send of a confirmation draft stays. A caller who opted out gets no reply or call-back control. [web]
- **R-STAFF-10** Portal boundary. Staff screens never call portal routes or import src/portal. Sharing a document shows the API's sentence about what clients see. No screen says or implies that a client can see a document. [web source-scan test]

## 10. Phase 2-3 edge cases

E162–E200 belong to FUB parity (Part 3 §14; E194–E200: the People filters). The D2 cases (Gmail inbox sync and publishing) are E201–E229. E230–E233 (the FUB migration owner decisions of 2026-11-11) are in Part 3 §14 too. E283 (an Admin turns call reviews off, owner decision 2026-11-11) is listed with the coaching cases. The D5 cases are E290–E309 (E290–E299 photo and video texts, E300–E309 reactions). E310–E312 (owner decision D8 of 2026-11-12: a person's reply in the website chat is first contact, R-OWN-14) and E341–E343 (owner decision D8 of 2026-11-12: a new inquiry from a person an import left unassigned is routed, R-OWN-20) follow them; E313–E340, E352–E354 and E590–E597 (module 22's lead intake: the Lead sources page, Google Ads lead forms, tracking numbers, portal email forwarding; R-LSRC, R-GADS, R-TRK, R-LEM-9..12), E380–E389 (the telephony safeguards; R-TWL), E360–E377, E600–E619 and E620–E639 (owner decision D9 of 2026-11-12: the in-house CMA; E600–E619 its V2-A sharing, E620–E639 lane V2-B's in-house home values and seller value ranges), E400–E419 (module 42, documents: R-DOC-5…10), E420–E439 (module 48, voice answering API: R-VOICE-11…24), E440–E459 (module 52, Google Ads: R-ADS-7…13), E460–E474 (modules 53 and 54, Google Business Profile and coaching trends: R-GBP-3…5, R-COACH-8), E475–E489 (pilot deploy readiness: database roles, TLS and the app VM; R-DEPLOY-1..7), E490–E539 (owner decision D12, proposed: the AI ISA, R-ISA-*; module 75 and the core and seller lanes), E540–E559 (the backlog-gaps lane: listings on member removal, the merge guard, merged work, home-value retention, reactions in the merge window, the reminder race, honest-UI fixes), E560–E589 (owner decision D10: the homeowner profile, R-HOME-*), E598–E599 with E640–E653 (task 55: MLS size is finished living area only when the MLS confirms its field, and PostalCode as sent; R-MLS-7; numbered E598–E599 and E640–E653 on its branch, E600–E613 renumbered at batch 13 because E600–E639 are the CMA V2 lanes') and E660–E679 (module 55, the Phase 3 staff screens: R-STAFF-1…10) close this table. E680–E699 are module 45, the client portal API (R-PORTAL-8…19).

| # | Case | Handling | Rules |
|---|---|---|---|
| E71 | Facebook DM from someone unknown | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): Messenger/Instagram DMs are out of scope; nothing is received from Messenger.** Old handling: Intake creates "Unknown (Messenger)" with the connection's lead source, routes it; the 60-s reply goes out in Messenger (step channel `inbound_channel`, part of the approved content) | R-INBOX-1, R-INBOX-4 |
| E72 | Agent replies on Messenger 25 h after the client's last message | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no Messenger replies and no platform window are built.** Old handling: `PLATFORM_WINDOW_CLOSED` before any provider call; compose offered SMS/email if allowed | R-INBOX-6 |
| E73 | Lead texted STOP last week, now writes on Instagram | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no Instagram inbound exists.** Old handling: SMS stays revoked; Instagram is a separate channel and the person wrote first, so a person can answer there; automation is paused by the inbound | R-CHAN-4, R-INBOX-2 |
| E74 | "Please stop contacting me" arrives on Messenger | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no Messenger inbound exists; the same language by email, web chat or synced Gmail is E155/E213.** Old handling: Person-level do-not-contact + every channel on every point revoked (`needs_review`); queued sends canceled; the handback says what happened | R-CHAN-3, R-CHAN-2 |
| E75 | Messenger profile name matches an existing contact | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no Messenger identities are created.** Old handling: Never merged automatically; suggestion card; merge through R-OWN-24 | R-INBOX-1 |
| E76 | The same person messages two of the team's Facebook Pages; STOP on one | Two identities on one contact; the DB guard refuses DMs to both. D2: no DMs exist; the 0018 guard branch and its smoke check stay until the cleanup | R-INBOX-3, R-CHAN-4 |
| E77 | Chat visitor closes the tab mid-conversation | Session idle 30 min ⇒ ended; chat sends to the closed session are denied `CHANNEL_NOT_CONNECTED`; nothing automated goes to the phone or email the visitor typed; the lead card shows it as "typed in chat, not verified" and the agent may add it and call or text. A reply being written when the chat ends is never moved to another channel silently: the box says the chat ended, that the text is still there and where Send goes now, seen and announced (batch 6 review) | R-INBOX-5 |
| E78 | Zillow changes its lead email template | Unparsed fields stay empty; `needs_review` (`PARTIAL_FORMAT`/`UNKNOWN_FORMAT`) with the email encrypted; nothing routes until an Owner/Admin/Team Lead accepts it (the reviewer may type a missing phone/email). 2026-11-11 lead-email slice: an unknown or partial format is always held, even with a phone found (owner requirement) | R-INBOX-9, R-LEM-5 |
| E79 | Same lead arrives by email parse and partner feed | Dedup by `(source, external_id)` or the 10-minute fingerprint | R-OWN-25, R-INBOX-9 |
| E80 | A DM written before the guard knows the channel | Impossible: the channel CHECK and the guard map changed in one migration; an unknown channel cannot be stored, and the guard raises `CHANNEL_UNKNOWN`. D2: applies to every channel; no DM channel is offered | R-GATE-13 |
| E81 | MLS feed contains the team's own listing | Linked by `team_listing_id`; team row authoritative; status mismatch shown; counted once | R-MLS-5 |
| E82 | MLS listing withdrawn while its alert is scheduled | Send-time recheck ⇒ `skipped_ineligible` | R-NURT-4 |
| E83 | Price drops, then rises inside the digest window | The message reflects the current price; no net drop ⇒ skipped | R-NURT-3, R-NURT-4 |
| E84 | Five saved homes drop price the same day | One digested message listing five homes | R-NURT-3 |
| E85 | MLS feed down for three days | Connection `degraded`; seller updates say "MLS data as of …"; MLS-sourced alerts and firings pause | R-MLS-3 |
| E86 | Listing broker opts a listing out of internet display | Never shown to clients (alerts, updates, portal); pending alerts canceled | R-MLS-2 |
| E87 | A week with zero showings logged | The update says "No showings logged in Realty OS this week"; the agent may skip it | R-SELL-2 |
| E88 | Agent taps Send without a recommendation | `SELLER_UPDATE_NEEDS_RECOMMENDATION`; the DB refuses an approval without `edited_payload.recommendation` | R-SELL-3, R-SELL-4 |
| E89 | Buyer-agent feedback is rude or names a buyer | Shown verbatim to the agent with a remove control; never auto-sent or rule-published | R-SELL-2, R-PORTAL-3 |
| E90 | Price changes after the website page was approved | The page shows the new price (bound fact); no re-approval; prose unchanged | R-MKT-3 |
| E91 | Listing withdrawn with invites unsent | Invites canceled; page 410; exports flagged | R-MKT-9, R-MKT-7 |
| E92 | Pack requires the brokerage name at least as prominent as the team name and the flyer fails | Cannot approve; the reason is shown | R-MKT-1 |
| E93 | Draft says "perfect for young families" | Hard match ⇒ approval blocked until edited; "family room" is not flagged | R-FH-1, R-FH-5 |
| E94 | An invitee unsubscribes between approval and send | That recipient is blocked; "Sent 41 of 45; 4 opted out"; no `failed_send` items | R-MKT-8 |
| E95 | Template puts the inspection deadline on a holiday | Proposed date shown with the rule used; never silently moved; a person confirms | R-TXN-1, R-TXN-6 |
| E96 | Closing date moved by amendment | `due_version` bumps; firings and drafts citing the old version go stale; one update with the new date | R-TXN-5, R-TXN-4 |
| E97 | Lender's email is unsubscribed | Lender update blocked; `failed_send` to the deal owner/TC | R-TXN-4, R-GATE-1 |
| E98 | AVM value 30 % below last month | Jump check ⇒ draft for the agent | R-AVM-2 |
| E99 | AVM has no value for a condo | `skipped_ineligible` with "No estimate available"; never "unchanged" | R-AVM-2 |
| E100 | Rates move 30 bp | **Retired: rate moves are deferred (R-NURT-6).** When they land: one feed card per agent citing the series, a draft per contact only on tap | R-NURT-6 |
| E101 | Home anniversary on Feb 29 | Fires Feb 28 in common years | R-NURT-2 |
| E102 | Agent revokes the calendar app in Google | Link `revoked`; in-app appointments unaffected; reconnect card | R-CAL-4 |
| E103 | Agent edits the synced event in Google | Detected at the next push of that appointment (etag mismatch); detached, never overwritten; "Edited in Google; times may differ" | R-CAL-2 |
| E104 | Imported FUB recording | Never reviewed | R-COACH-1 |
| E105 | A rubric quote fails verification | Item `needs_review`; excluded from the denominator | R-COACH-2 |
| E106 | Agent has two scored calls in 14 days | No daily fix ("Not enough calls yet") | R-COACH-4 |
| E283 | An Admin turns call reviews off (owner decision 2026-11-11: Owner on, Admin off, as Gmail sync, except that the system cannot turn call reviews off and an Admin's off needs no re-auth) | Allowed without re-auth: open tips close at once, audited like the Owner's change. Turning them on, Team Lead visibility and the checklist stay the Owner's: an Admin's request that names any of them (even as stored), alone or together with turning reviews off, is refused whole (`OWNER_REQUIRED`; DB `OWNER_ONLY_SETTING`). The off sticks: each request names only the settings it changes and is decided on the settings row as it is (locked), so an Owner's checklist or Team Lead save from a page loaded earlier leaves reviews off, and turning them back on asks for the Owner's password against the row as it is | R-COACH-1, R-COACH-3 |
| E107 | Closed deal without commission | Excluded from dollars, listed as "missing commission" | R-OWNER-1 |
| E108 | A source with cost and zero closings | "— (0 closings)", never $0 or ∞ | R-OWNER-2 |
| E109 | Fewer than 10 deals entered a stage in the last year | That stage is left out of the forecast and counted ("not enough history") | R-OWNER-3 |
| E110 | Agent has no goals | The plan is built from the pipeline only and says so | R-PLAN-1 |
| E111 | The plan cites a deal reassigned since | Item dropped at render | R-PLAN-3 |
| E112 | After-hours call from a number that asked not to be called | Answered (they called); no callback offered by phone; confirmation only on an allowed channel | R-VOICE-6, R-GATE-14 |
| E113 | Caller asks "Am I talking to a real person?" | The scripted truthful answer | R-VOICE-8 |
| E114 | Caller asks what the seller would take | Declined politely; message taken; money handback | R-VOICE-5 |
| E115 | The contact's owner does not answer within the 20 s hold | Back to the caller; message taken or booked; critical handback and a promise task | R-VOICE-6, R-VOICE-7 |
| E116 | Caller in an all-party recording state | Disclosure + announcement before any processing; decline ⇒ voicemail | R-VOICE-2 |
| E117 | Voice vendor outage | Voicemail fallback | R-VOICE-10 |
| E118 | Two calls at once from the same unknown number | Address lock ⇒ one contact | R-OWN-25 |
| E119 | Caller speaks a language the playbook does not cover | Takes a short message or goes to voicemail, and says so | R-VOICE-5 |
| E120 | Contact has a self-reported PCS timeline | Not representable in the prediction feature type (compile-time test) | R-PRED-1 |
| E121 | Contact flagged "likely" | Feed card only; no message is sent because of it | R-PRED-4 |
| E122 | Public record owner name differs from the contact | Not attached; needs review | R-PRED-2 |
| E123 | Reallocation proposed while at the cap | Moves money between campaigns only; the tenant-wide total stays ≤ the ceiling in force | R-ADS-2 |

| E131 | Extraction quotes a closing date that is not in the PDF | Needs review; never a milestone until confirmed (module 42: `documents/decide.ts` verifies the quote at its page's character range; the confirm is `EXTRACTION_CLAIM_NEEDS_REVIEW` and 0069 `app.deal_dates_evidence_guard` refuses the date) | R-DOC-2, R-DOC-8, R-TXN-1 |

| E133 | Negative review | Reply drafted on tap only; review requests were never gated by sentiment | R-GBP-1, R-MKT-11 |
| E134 | A Page is disconnected and reconnected in the same tenant | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no Messenger/Instagram identities exist; a Page is a publishing connection only.** Old handling: Same HMACs: identities, history and opt-outs intact; sends were `CHANNEL_NOT_CONNECTED` meanwhile | R-CHAN-1, R-INBOX-7 |
| E135 | A Page moves from tenant A to tenant B | Live in one tenant at a time; B connects it only through the provider callback (proof of control); A's opt-outs stay in A (R-CONSENT-20); in B the window lets the team reply only to people who write first; the connect dialog says so. D2: a Page is a publishing connection only (R-MKT-12); the window part is gone (R-INBOX-6 retired), the one-tenant rule stands | R-INT-3 |
| E136 | Meta webhook for a Page with no connected or degraded connection | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): the product receives no Meta webhooks.** Old handling: `unrouted`, platform alert, nothing delivered | R-TEN-11 |
| E137 | 5,000 chat messages from one IP in a minute | Rate limits then 429; no contact, offer or notification | R-INBOX-5 |
| E138 | A chat visitor types an email matching an owned contact | Never attached: a new contact with only the chat identity, plus a duplicate suggestion "typed in chat, not verified" to the owner; the visitor sees only this session's replies; merging is a human decision (R-OWN-24). Merged as suggested (the earlier record survives), the chat moves with the person: the survivor answers it while it is open, and the merged-away record shows no chat (batch 6 review) | R-INBOX-1, R-INBOX-5, R-TEN-12 |
| E139 | A person-level do-not-contact contact DMs the Page | **Retired: owner decision D2 (2026-11-11): no DMs are received; a do-not-contact contact who writes by email, web chat or synced Gmail is handled the same way.** Old handling: Inbound stored, pause, handback; every reply blocked `PERSON_DO_NOT_CONTACT`; the thread says so | R-CHAN-2 |
| E140 | A Messenger-only contact merges into an SMS contact; one side is do-not-contact | The survivor keeps the earliest `do_not_contact_at` (DB trigger, along merge chains); identities move; pause and opt-outs win; an address added to the survivor later is blocked too. Its chat sessions move with the identity (batch 6 review), so the survivor's summary says the chat is there and, with the do-not-contact, blocked | R-CHAN-2, R-OWN-24 |
| E141 | The MLS sends a modification older than the stored one | Refused (`MLS_STALE_UPDATE`); the sync skips it | R-MLS-1 |

| E161 | An Owner approves a shift; the job replays the approval the next day | The second planned row is refused (approval single-use) | R-ADS-2 |
| E201 | A client's Reply-All reaches both platform inbound and the agent's Gmail | Stored and acted on once when the platform row carries the Message-ID hash: the platform row wins; the Gmail copy is refused, or deleted when the platform row lands after it. One handback: both paths use the dedupe key `email:{contact}:{rfc_message_id_sha256}` when the Message-ID hash is known, so whichever lands first hands back and the other does not. A platform row without the hash is not deduplicated, so a backfill can show that message twice: a row saved before module 22 writes the hash (the `platform:gmail` rollout precondition waits such rows out of the backfill window, or a one-time repair hashes them) and imported email (never hashed) | R-GMAIL-12, R-GMAIL-10 |
| E202 | A client writes from an address not on file | Not stored; the agent may add the address to the contact by hand and confirm it (one-tap suggestion), and later mail syncs (earlier mail only through "import again", inside the window) | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E203 | The agent's personal mail (family, doctor, bank) | Never stored: only addresses the agent confirmed for sync (`mail_sync_points`) are matched, so a peer who adds a stranger's address to a contact and makes the agent a collaborator or deal member, or a TL who adds the agent's own mailbox as a contact point, captures nothing; a live mailbox address of the team is never a client address (`MAIL_ADDRESS_IS_A_MAILBOX`) | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E204 | A client is Cc'd on a thread with a lender who is not a contact | Stored for the client (role `cc`); the lender's address is never stored ("and 1 other") | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E205 | The agent Bcc's a client | Stored with role `bcc`; the other recipients only as a count | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E206 | The agent forwards a client's email to a personal friend | No contact address on the forward ⇒ not stored | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E207 | A client forwards a third party's email | Stored as the client's message; quoted history stripped best effort | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E208 | A contract PDF is attached | Metadata only (name, type, size), never fetched; the agent uploads it to documents if wanted | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E209 | A 500-message thread | At most `max_messages_per_thread` copies; the timeline says older messages stay in Gmail | R-GMAIL-5 |
| E210 | A shared mailbox a teammate already linked | Refused (one live link per mailbox) | R-GMAIL-2 |
| E211 | The same Gmail connected in a second workspace | Refused (one live link per mailbox across all tenants) | R-GMAIL-2 |
| E212 | The agent removes a thread | Hidden at once, deleted within one sync interval, later messages never stored; no un-exclude | R-GMAIL-7 |
| E213 | "Please stop emailing me" sent to the agent's Gmail | Person-level do-not-contact (`needs_review`), the same as platform email | R-GMAIL-9 |
| E214 | An old "unsubscribe" inside the backfill | Revokes email on that point | R-GMAIL-9 |
| E215 | The agent leaves the team | Removed: link revoked, token deleted, copies hidden at once and deleted (owner question Q-GMAIL-1, default delete). Suspended: the link is paused, not revoked; copies hidden until reactivation, then sync resumes | R-GMAIL-13, R-INT-5 |
| E216 | A contact is reassigned A → B | B sees A's earlier synced mail like the timeline; A's new mail with that contact is no longer stored | R-GMAIL-6, R-GMAIL-5 |
| E217 | The agent revokes access in their Google account | `invalid_grant` ⇒ `PROVIDER_REVOKED`, handled as a disconnect | R-GMAIL-13 |
| E218 | The stored history id has expired | Bounded resync inside the backfill window; never a full-mailbox read; a live message it finds that is older than 1 hour is stored and opt-out-classified but neither pauses nor hands back | R-GMAIL-11, R-GMAIL-10 |
| E219 | A message is trashed in Gmail | Its copies are deleted in the next pass | R-GMAIL-11 |
| E220 | A support actor under an Owner grant opens the contact | No synced mail (RESTRICTIVE) | R-GMAIL-6 |
| E221 | The client portal | Synced mail never appears (Layer 1 staff only) | R-GMAIL-6 |
| E222 | The Owner turns Gmail sync off | Every copy hidden at once; links revoked in the next reconcile pass and copies deleted within one sync interval | R-GMAIL-1, R-GMAIL-13 |
| E223 | Two contacts are merged | Copies re-pointed to the survivor and its confirmed email point with the same address HMAC (system only); a copy that would collide with the survivor's own copy of the same Gmail message, or whose survivor point is unconfirmed, is deleted instead | R-GMAIL-13 |
| E224 | A contact is erased | The contact's copies are deleted | R-GMAIL-13 |
| E225 | The token also grants `gmail.modify` (incremental authorization) | Refused by the callback; nothing stored (scopes CHECK) | R-GMAIL-3 |
| E226 | A live reply from the client in Gmail | Automation paused; exactly one handback "replied in Gmail", citing the copy by id and time (no snippet), when the message is at most 1 hour old at fetch | R-GMAIL-10 |
| E227 | A Facebook publish times out | `unknown`, reconciled by lookup, never re-posted; the UI never says "Posted" until the provider confirms | R-MKT-12 |
| E228 | Someone DMs the connected Facebook Page | Nothing is received (no messaging webhook); DMs are out of scope (D2) | R-MKT-12 |
| E229 | A client writes "unsubscribe" in a thread the agent excluded | The opt-out is recorded (consent event citing `gmail_message_ref`); nothing is stored | R-GMAIL-9, R-GMAIL-7 |
| E290 | The client texts STOP, or a do-not-contact is recorded, after a photo text was scheduled | STOP cancels it (cancelQueuedSmsTo covers mms); otherwise the send-time check blocks it. Nothing reaches the provider and no media URL is minted. The bubble keeps its photos and says why | R-MMS-5, R-CONSENT-7 |
| E291 | A send names an upload that is someone else's, another person's or another tenant's, or that is expired, removed or already sent; or two sends race for one upload | 409 `MEDIA_UPLOAD_GONE`, identical in every case, and nothing is created; of two racing sends, one wins. The guard refuses a direct attach | R-MMS-2, R-MMS-3 |
| E292 | A bulk text, approved draft, assistant/playbook/stage-automation step, saved-search alert, STOP/HELP reply, agent alert or marketing send tries to carry media | No input accepts media (the bulk fields give `media_one_to_one_only`). The database refuses an automated `mms` row and any attach to one | R-MMS-1 |
| E293 | A video over 500 KB, media plus text over 5 MB, an 11th item or a text over 1,600 characters; or the carrier refuses the media anyway | Refused in the box, in words (with the file's size, in decimal units), before upload, and again by the API (`MEDIA_TOO_LARGE` or a validation error); for a long video the box says to send a link instead. A carrier refusal (11751, 30019, 12300) ends "Not delivered: …" with the reason in words | R-MMS-3, R-MMS-8, R-MMS-9 |
| E294 | A file claims to be a photo or video but is HTML, SVG, PDF, HEIC, audio, a disguised MP4 brand, or a structure the strip cannot walk | `MEDIA_NOT_ALLOWED`; nothing is stored; the declared type is never trusted | R-MMS-3, R-MMS-4 |
| E295 | A photo or video carries GPS location (in Exif, XMP, a segment between scans, a secondary image, data after the end marker, or a video metadata box) | Stored and sent without it, with the photo keeping its orientation.
