| E686 | A phone number while texting cannot really send (no Twilio, live texting off), or a channel that is a fake outside tests | 503 PORTAL_NOT_AVAILABLE {channel} for every such address; no token | R-PORTAL-10 |
| E687 | Unsafe client route with no, a wrong, or the staff session's CSRF token | 403 CSRF_INVALID | R-PORTAL-9 |
| E688 | Another grant's, deal's or tenant's id in timeline, documents or stream-token | 404 | R-PORTAL-2, R-PERM-5 |
| E689 | Stream token reused or over 60 s, or the document unshared, item retracted or grant revoked before download | 404, nothing streamed | R-PORTAL-7, R-PORTAL-17 |
| E690 | Invite a non-party, or by an ISA, TC, support or another agent | 409 PORTAL_NOT_A_PARTY; 404 without the action | R-PORTAL-13 |
| E691 | Invite a contact with no active email or phone; the point later removed or marked wrong | 409 PORTAL_LOGIN_POINT_MISSING; later the staff view says sign-in is impossible and offers another point | R-PORTAL-13 |
| E692 | Invite twice, or two taps at once | Same account and grant; no second notice unless resend; the answer never claims a note went when none did (b14 audit F7): a note sent ⇒ "already invited", one on its way ⇒ says so, none sent yet by this inviter (invited while the portal was off) ⇒ the note goes now, else ⇒ none reached them, Resend | R-PORTAL-13 |
| E693 | Invite while uncleared | Account and grant created, nothing sent; response and preview say so | R-PORTAL-13, R-INT-6 |
| E694 | Publish an unshared, pending, infected or contact-scoped document, or a proposed milestone | Core refuses before the DB; a forged insert is refused by the guard | R-PORTAL-3, R-PORTAL-14 |
| E695 | Showing, feedback or seller update through the deal's listing to a buyer-side client, or a seller who is only the primary contact | 409 PORTAL_NOT_SELLER_SIDE in core and DB; the sentence says to add them as seller | R-PORTAL-19 |
| E696 | A published milestone moves, is waived or goes back to proposed; or a person had retracted it | Sync retracts it and, for a move, publishes the new version, unless a person retracted any version | R-PORTAL-15 |
| E697 | A person retracts by mistake | They publish the source again; the old row stays retracted | R-PORTAL-14 |
| E698 | The deal closes or is lost; a grant is made after closing | Expiry stored once, 90 days after the later of closing and the grant, or at once when lost; nothing visible after | R-PORTAL-15, R-PORTAL-6 |
| E699 | Pack withdrawn mid-session or between stream token and download | Next request 401; download refused | R-INT-6, R-PORTAL-7 |

---

# Part 3: FUB parity (migration `0021_fub_parity.sql`, renumbered at merge)

Part 3 covers the everyday contact-management jobs agents do in Follow Up Boss that Realty OS lacked. The gap analysis is `docs/FUB_PARITY.md`, the build is `docs/PARITY_PLAN.md`, and the proof is `packages/db/test/rls_smoke_parity.sql`. It merges two independent drafts (`docs/parity/draft-a.md`, simplicity-first; `docs/parity/draft-b.md`, risk-first) after a security critic and a simplicity critic (ARCHITECTURE §20, "FUB parity design review").

Parts 1 and 2 stay in force and nothing here weakens them:
- D1 opt-outs are checked in `canContact` and both DB guards, with no override and no bulk exemption.
- FORCE RLS with `(tenant_id, id)` keys.
- Ownership changes only through `applyOwnershipCommand`.
- Every send goes through `dispatchOutbound`.
- The assistant acts on its own only inside approved playbooks.

## 11. Parity tables

### 11.1 New tables (10)

| Table | Purpose | Key columns | Constraints / triggers | RLS (see §12) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `tags` | Tenant tag catalog | id, name (trimmed, 1–64), **name_key** (generated `lower(normalize(name, NFKC))`, UQ per tenant), color (12 palette tokens or NULL), protected_proxy, created_by_kind (`user/import`), created_by_user_id | ≤ 1,000 per tenant (advisory-locked count); lifting `protected_proxy` is Owner-only | read: members; create: members except TC, as self, or system (import); rename/recolor/protect: Owner/Admin/system; delete: Owner/Admin |
| `contact_tags` | The one source of a person's tags | (tenant_id, contact_id, tag_id) PK, added_by_kind (`user/bulk/import/migrated`), added_by_user_id, bulk_action_id, import_batch_id | kind ⇔ provenance column; ≤ 100 per person; contact and tag cascade | read: **contact detail visible**; write: detail visible + as self + not TC, or system |
| `contact_stage_events` | Append-only stage history | id, contact_id, from_stage, to_stage, actor_kind, actor_user_id, occurred_at, bulk_action_id (0041: the bulk stage action that made the change) | written only by the definer trigger `app.contacts_stage_history` on `contacts.stage` change; `bulk_action_id` only when the transaction names (`app.bulk_action_id`) a running stage action of the tenant created by the user making the change, else NULL; UPDATE refused; cascades with the contact | read: contact detail visible; app_user cannot insert |
| `custom_field_defs` | Tenant-defined typed fields | id, key (`^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{1,40}$`, UQ, immutable), label (UQ live, case-insensitive), field_type (`text/number/date/single_select/multi_select`, immutable), options `[{id,label,archived?}]` (≤ 100, ids unique, never removed), annual (date only), position, origin (`manual/fub_import/csv_import`), archived_at | ≤ **50 live** per tenant (advisory-locked); key ∉ the built-in protected attribute keys (R-AST-12 list); a name matching the fair-housing lexicon is refused by core (R-PAR-18) | read: members; write: Owner/Admin, or system for import origins |
| `contact_field_values` | One typed value per person per field | (tenant_id, contact_id, field_id) PK, exactly one of value_text (≤ 1,000) / value_number / value_date / value_options, updated_by_kind (`user/bulk/import`), updated_by_user_id, import_batch_id | type matches the def; options are live ids, distinct, single select = 1; archived field read-only | read: contact detail visible; write: as `contact_tags` |
| `contact_relationships` | Links between people | id, contact_id, related_contact_id, kind (`spouse_partner/family/household/co_buyer/co_seller/referred_by/other`), label (`other` only), created_by_user_id | not self; one row per unordered pair and kind; `referred_by` directional | read/insert/delete: **both** people detail-visible (+ not TC to write); no update |
| `message_templates` | Email and text templates | id, scope (`team/personal`), owner_user_id, channel (`sms/email`), name (UQ live per scope/owner/channel), subject (email only, no CR/LF), body (SMS ≤ 1,600, email ≤ 20,000), version, merge_fields (closed set, regex CHECK), updated_by_user_id, archived_at | scope/owner/channel immutable; content change ⇔ version + 1 | team: members read, Owner/Admin write; personal: the owner only (Owner/Admin do not read it); system reads all |
| `smart_lists` | Saved filter definitions | id, scope, owner_user_id, name, definition jsonb (≤ 8 KB, core Zod v1), definition_version, sort (5 values), position, archived_at | scope/owner immutable | team: members read, Owner/Admin/TL write; personal: the owner |
| `bulk_actions` | One audited bulk job | id, kind (`tag/untag/stage/assign/enroll/email/text`), status (`draft → confirmed → running → completed/canceled/failed`), created_by_user_id + created_by_role (= the creator's role at insert), source (`selection/smart_list` + smart_list_id), params (ids/codes), template_id + version, subject/body snapshot (frozen), target_count, counts, idempotency_key (UQ), lifecycle instants, failure_code | born draft; only the creator writes a draft's content and, in the same role, confirms; the confirmed count must equal the frozen items (so caps hold); kind/creator/params immutable; content and target frozen after draft; `assign` only Owner/Admin/TL; no TC; text ≤ 200 and confirmed only while the switch is on | read: creator, tenant-wide, creator's TL; write: creator as self, Owner/Admin (cancel), system |
| `bulk_action_items` | One person in a bulk job | (tenant_id, bulk_action_id, contact_id) PK, status (`pending/done/skipped/blocked/failed/canceled`), reason_code, address_hmac (UQ per action), message_id, processed_at | added only while the action is a draft; a send item names its address; person, action and address never change; a processed item is final | read: a visible action **and** the person's detail visible to the reader; insert: the creator, person detail-visible; update: creator or system |

### 11.2 Changed tables

| Table | Change |
|---|---|
| `contact_points` | + `removed_reason` (`replaced/old/wrong_number`, set by the action, never asked for; `wrong_number` only when the consent ledger holds a `wrong_number` revoke for that address). Trigger `contact_points_immutable`: contact, kind, address and HMAC never change; a removal reason never changes; a confirmed wrong number is never restored. Adding an address the person once had restores that row (same id and HMAC history); a confirmed wrong number cannot be added back either (one row per person and address), so the API answers `WRONG_NUMBER_FINAL`. |
| `contact_addresses` | (migration 0037) `app_user` may DELETE, only under the RESTRICTIVE `contact_addresses_delete` policy: the person's detail visible and a user actor in an editing role (never a TC, the system or support). Module 57's audited `DELETE /contacts/:id/addresses/:addressId` also checks `contact.edit`. Its Undo re-inserts the deleted row verbatim (same id, jurisdiction, import batch) from a token the delete sealed with the tenant's field key, for the same user and person within 10 minutes (`POST .../restore`); never from fields the client sends. |
| `messages_dispatch_guard`, `calls_dispatch_guard` (replaced) | The "any of the contact's phones" opt-out fan-out also reads **removed** points, unless removed as a confirmed wrong number (R-PAR-12). This only refuses more sends; the rest is verbatim from 0018. |
| `notes` | + edited_at, activity_kind (`call/text/email`; meetings use appointments), activity_direction, activity_at, activity_outcome (calls only); `source` + `logged_activity`. Shape CHECKs; activity_at ≤ created_at + 5 min and ≥ created_at − 366 d. Trigger `notes_edit_guard`: only the author changes a body or a logged time/direction/outcome (imported notes and call summaries never); parent, author, source and kind never change. New `notes_delete` policy + DELETE grant. There is no mentions table: mentions are checked at save (R-PAR-2). |
| `notifications.kind` | + `mention`, `bulk_finished` |
| `contacts` | `tags` retired: backfilled into `tags`/`contact_tags` (first spelling wins). The import lane drops the column (R-PAR-10). Triggers `contacts_stage_history` (definer) and `contacts_stage_guard` (R-PAR-14). Five list indexes on live contacts (stage, last activity, created, source) + `tasks_open_contact_due_idx`. |
| `tenant_settings` | + `stage_labels` jsonb (keys ⊆ the eight stages, values 1–24 chars, trigger); + `batch_text_enabled` (default false; only the Owner turns it on, trigger) |
| `playbooks` | + `auto_enroll_stage` (one active playbook per scope/owner/stage; not for `new_lead_response`) |
| `playbook_enrollments.enrolled_by` | + `stage_change`, `bulk` (bulk ⇒ enrolled_by_user_id) |
| `memberships` | + `email_signature` (plain text ≤ 1,000, no control characters; self-editable) |
| `messages` | + bulk_action_id (FK, set at insert, never changed); CHECK: a bulk message is human, outbound, marketing, SMS or email; trigger `messages_bulk_guard`: the action is running, of the message's channel, the sender is its creator, the person has a **pending** item whose frozen address is the recipient, and a text needs the switch on |
| views | `contact_timeline` adds stage events and shows a logged activity at the time it happened (`detail = 'logged_activity:<kind>'`); `v_agent_activity_daily` excludes bulk messages from touches. Same columns. |

## 12. Parity RLS and capability matrix

Every new table gets the following:
- Layer 1: the RESTRICTIVE staff-only `tenant_isolation`, plus `support_read_only_{ins,upd,del}`.
- FORCE RLS.
- The `support_read_only` statement trigger.

The role policies are in §11.1. Two rules carry the design:
- **A person's child rows follow the person.** Tags, field values, stage history and notes are visible exactly when `app.contact_detail_visible` is true, so a pond or offer card never shows them.
- **Shared things are definitions, not data.** A team list or team template is visible to members, but running it reads only the viewer's own contacts.

| Capability (new `core.can()` action) | Owner | Admin | Team Lead | Agent | ISA | TC |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `contact.edit` (names, type, intent, stage, points, addresses, tags, custom values, relationships) | ✅ | ✅ | T | O | O | — |
| `note.write` (own notes; mentions) | ✅ | ✅ | T + pond detail | O + D | O | D |
| `note.delete` (own; any for ✅) | ✅ | ✅ | own | own | own | own |
| `activity.log` | ✅ | ✅ | T | O | O | — |
| `tag.create` (by typing) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | — |
| `tag.manage` (rename, merge, color, protect, delete) | ✅ | ✅ | — | — | — | — |
| `custom_field.manage` | ✅ | ✅ | — | — | — | — |
| `settings.stage_labels` | ✅ | ✅ | — | — | — | — |
| `template.personal` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| `template.team_manage` | ✅ | ✅ | — | — | — | — |
| `smart_list.personal` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ (D) |
| `smart_list.team_manage` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | — | — | — |
| `bulk.run` (tag, untag, stage, enroll, email) | ✅ | ✅ | T | O | O | — |
| `bulk.assign` | ✅ | ✅ | T (to team members) | — | — | — |
| `bulk.text` (also needs the switch) | ✅ | ✅ | T | O | O | — |
| `settings.batch_text` (turn on) | ✅ 🔒 | off only | — | — | — | — |
| `playbook.auto_enroll_stage` | team ✅ | team ✅ | — | own agent playbook | — | — |
| `collaborator.manage` (existing RLS) | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ | contact owner | — | — |

## 13. FUB parity business rules

### Notes, mentions, logged activity
- **R-PAR-1** A note is readable by exactly whoever sees its person's detail (or its deal, for a deal-only note); there are no private notes. The UI says "Everyone who can see Jane can read this note." [DB, smoke]
- **R-PAR-2** A mention notifies a user only if that user's **own** RLS shows the note's person (or deal). While saving the note, the API checks each mentioned active member in a read-only transaction in that member's own context; no definer re-implements visibility for another user. A mention never grants access. A user who cannot see the person reads neither the note nor the notification. The author is told at once: "Sam can't see Jane, so they weren't told", with a **Share** action (adds a collaborator under existing RLS, audited). The mention picker shows who can see the person. There is no mentions table: the text-free notification is the record. [DB, smoke + api test]
- **R-PAR-3** A mention notification never contains note text. Its title holds at most the person's first name and initial (tenant `notification_detail`), and its dedupe key is `mention:{note}` per user. A note mentions at most 10 people; a self-mention is ignored. An edit notifies only newly added people. [smoke + api snapshot test]
- **R-PAR-4** Only the author edits a note's body, which sets `edited_at`. Imported notes and call summaries are never edited. The author deletes their own manual or logged note; a tenant-wide role deletes any note, audited as `note.delete`. Owner/Admin may pin or unpin. A deleted note's evidence refs read `source_available = false`. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PAR-5** Notes, mentions and logged activities never count as a touch (R-OWN-14 unchanged). [core + view test]
- **R-PAR-30** A person logs a call, text or email that happened outside Realty OS as a note with `source = 'logged_activity'` and its kind, direction, time and (calls) outcome. It is never a `messages` or `calls` row, and it is labelled "Logged by <name>" everywhere, at the time it happened. "Log a meeting" is the existing past appointment + outcome. The log sheet offers "Remind me in N days", which creates an ordinary task. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PAR-31** A logged activity's time is at most 5 minutes ahead of when it was entered and at most 366 days back. The author may correct the time, direction and outcome; the kind and the person never change. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PAR-32** A logged activity never satisfies or delays first-touch SLA, release or inactivity release, never clears an exception and never appears in touch counts. The Team Lead's exception card shows logged activities in the period as context. Logging "they asked not to be contacted" offers the one-tap person-level do-not-contact (the R-CHAN-3 path); it never only writes a note. [view + core + web]
  - *Implementation note (lead-journey fix, J5).* An `untouched_lead` item's evidence lists the activities logged in its own ownership period (from the minute the period started, since the log sheet records minutes, until the period ended: the lead left its owner, or a re-inquiry started the same owner's next period, R-OWN-20 / E68), read at read time under the viewer's rights, as "Logged by {who}: outgoing call, talked (context only; not a first contact)". The line is context only: the item's condition, the SLA, release and the scoreboard still read human outbound touches alone (`accountability/actions.test.ts`).

### Tags
- **R-PAR-6** Tag names are unique per tenant, case- and width-insensitively (`name_key`), and stored trimmed. The first spelling is kept for display. A tenant has at most 1,000 tags and a person at most 100. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PAR-7** A person's tags are visible exactly when their detail is. They are written by someone who sees that detail, as themselves, and who is not a TC (or by the system for imports). No tag changes whether anyone can be contacted. Adding a tag that reads like an opt-out (the importer's `TAG_DNC`/`TAG_OPT_OUT`/`TAG_UNSUB` patterns) prompts "Tags don't stop messages. Record 'do not contact' for Jane?", and removing such a tag never lifts anything. [DB, smoke + web]
- **R-PAR-8** A tag whose name matches the fair-housing protected-proxy lexicon (core module 20) is `protected_proxy`; Owner/Admin may protect any tag. Protection is tighten-only: only the Owner lifts it. A protected tag works for viewing and one-to-one work. It is never applied in bulk (DB), never shown on a pond card, never an assistant input, and never defines the audience of a bulk email, text or enrollment (`AUDIENCE_PROTECTED_PROXY`, no override), also not through a bulk stage change into a stage that starts a plan (the stage changes, the plan does not start: R-PAR-16). **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (which proxies, per state). [DB, smoke + core]
- **R-PAR-9** Members other than TCs create tags by typing them. Only Owner/Admin rename, recolor, protect, merge or delete. A rename is one row. A merge moves the source's links to the target in one transaction (ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING), rewrites smart lists that reference the source, takes the stricter protection, then deletes the source. All are audited (`tag.rename/merge/delete/protect`, names and counts). [DB, smoke + api]
- **R-PAR-10** `contacts.tags` is retired. The migration backfills it into `tags` + `contact_tags` (`migrated`). New code never reads it. The import lane moves its writer to `contact_tags`, and its migration then empties and drops the column. [api + migration test]

### People: add, edit, points
- **R-PAR-11** A contact point's contact, kind, address and HMAC never change. "Editing" a phone or email means removing it (with a reason) and adding the new one. Consent, suppressions and opt-outs stay keyed by HMAC, so the old address keeps its opt-out and the new one starts with whatever its own HMAC carries. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PAR-12** Removing a point never un-blocks the person. The "any of the person's phones" fan-out for SMS (and DMs) and the voice "any phone" check include removed points. The one exception is a point removed as a **confirmed wrong number**: the consent ledger must already hold a `wrong_number` revoke for that address, written by the call-outcome or wrong-number-reply path (platform email, or a reply found in synced Gmail, R-GMAIL-9). A status edit alone is not proof, and such a point is never restored. The UI explains any over-block: "Texting Jane is blocked because a number you removed (•••1234) opted out; she can text START from it." [DB guards, smoke + core]
- **R-PAR-43** `PATCH /contacts/:id` edits names, contact type, intent, timezone, stage and the pond summary (owner/TL), using an optimistic `row_version` (`STALE_CONTACT`). It never edits ownership, attribution, merge or delete state, `do_not_contact_*` or `imported_fields`; the strict Zod body and the existing guards refuse them. Edits are audited as `contact.update`, with field names only for PII. [api]
- **R-PAR-44** Manual create and adding a point dedup exactly as intake does (sorted per-HMAC locks, `find_contacts_by_address`). An exact match on create is `DUPLICATE_CONTACT` unless `confirmDistinct`. On add-point it is a warning plus a duplicate candidate. A match the caller cannot see reveals only its owner's display name (R-PERM-12). Create may set stage, tags, source (once, at birth) and an address. An Owner/Admin/TL "create for" is a create plus one `assign` command (two ledger events, no SLA). [api + concurrency test]
- **R-PAR-45** A merge moves the loser's tags, field values (the survivor's value wins when both are set), relationships (self-pairs dropped, pairs deduped) and stage history to the survivor, inside the engine's merge transaction. Notes and mentions already move with the contact's rows per the existing merge (VERIFY in `merge.ts`; extend it if not). [db merge test]

### Stages
- **R-PAR-13** Stages are the fixed eight (engine meaning: R-OWN-23 inactivity applies to `new`/`attempting`). Owner/Admin may give any of them a display label of 1–24 characters (`tenant_settings.stage_labels`). Code, lists and imports use the keys. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PAR-14** An Agent or ISA cannot move a routed lead with an unmet first-touch SLA (`first_touch_due_at` set, `first_touch_at` null) to any stage outside `new`/`attempting`, `lost` included (`STAGE_NEEDS_CONTACT`). This stops a lead being parked where the inactivity release does not apply. Giving up on such a lead is the Close ownership command, which ends the period instead of sheltering it. The stage picker shows these stages locked up front, "Reach them first: Call · Text · Close" (with Reply in chat first while the lead's website chat is open: since owner decision D8 a chat reply is first contact, R-OWN-14, and opens the stages like a call, text or email), never failing after the tap. A TL/Owner/Admin may move it, audited. Self-created contacts have no SLA and are unaffected. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PAR-15** Every stage change appends a `contact_stage_events` row, written only by the definer trigger and attributed to the actor of the transaction. It is visible with the person's detail, never changes, and appears in the timeline. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PAR-16** A playbook may name one stage whose entry enrolls the person (`auto_enroll_stage`; team playbooks are Owner/Admin, an agent's own playbook covers only their own contacts; one active playbook per scope/owner/stage). Enrollment happens only from an approved version, never when a live enrollment exists, never for a person who cannot be reached, and never from a stage change a bulk action made while that action's audience is built from a protected tag (R-PAR-8; skipped `protected_audience`, read when the plan would start; migration 0041). It goes through the existing enrollment path with `enrolled_by = 'stage_change'`, and a skipped enrollment is shown with its reason. A stage change by an import never auto-enrolls. [core + api]

### Custom fields and important dates
- **R-PAR-17** Custom fields are typed (text, number, date, single select, multi select). There are at most 50 live per tenant. Key and type never change. Options are archived, never removed, and an archived field is read-only. Only Owner/Admin (or an import, as the system) define them. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PAR-18** A custom field cannot recreate a built-in protected attribute (DB: the R-AST-12 key list). Core refuses a field whose label or options match the fair-housing lexicon (`CUSTOM_FIELD_PROTECTED`) and points to the built-in self-reported attributes (`contact_attributes`), which stay the only home for such facts. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL.** [DB, smoke + core]
- **R-PAR-19** A person's custom values are visible exactly when their detail is, and are written like tags (R-PAR-7). [DB, smoke]
- **R-PAR-20** An `annual` date field (birthday, home anniversary) drives "coming up in N days" list clauses and reminder tasks. Core computes the next occurrence in the tenant's timezone, and 29 February falls on 28 February in non-leap years. It never sends anything by itself; a send needs a stage-entry or manually enrolled playbook. [core]

### Relationships
- **R-PAR-21** A relationship exists for a viewer only when they see both people's detail. There is no "hidden relationship" count, and nobody links a person they cannot see. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PAR-22** A relationship grants no access, merges nothing, moves no opt-out and is never a merge-field source. FUB relationship contacts import as `imported_fields` text only: their phones and emails never become contact points. [api + import test]

### Templates and signature
- **R-PAR-23** Team templates are read by members and written by Owner/Admin. A personal template is its owner's alone (Owner/Admin do not read it). A TL copies a team template to personal to change it. [DB, smoke]
- **R-PAR-24** Merge fields are a closed set: `contact.first_name/last_name/display_name`, `agent.first_name/last_name/phone/email` (the sender's own), `team.name`, `brokerage.name`, and `field.<key>` for live custom fields (the DB CHECK allows only these shapes; core also ties them to the body and refuses archived keys). Fallbacks use `{{x|fallback}}`, with no logic. Rendering is a pure core function over a context built for **one** person under the sender's RLS, so a field the sender cannot see is absent. Output is plain text: values are inserted literally in one pass, control characters are stripped, and the subject has no CR/LF. A field with no value and no fallback refuses the send (`MERGE_FIELD_EMPTY`, never "Hi ,"); in bulk it skips that person. [DB CHECK, smoke + core property test]
- **R-PAR-25** The send path adds brokerage identity and the marketing footer (render.ts, R-GATE-12); a template cannot remove them. The sender's plain-text `email_signature` is appended by the renderer, never parsed as markup. The composer's existing `scheduleAt` becomes a "Send later" picker (one-to-one only). [api]
- **R-PAR-26** Every content change bumps a template's version. A bulk action freezes the version and the rendered source at confirm. Playbook steps never reference human templates, and a template never makes a send autonomous. [DB, smoke + architecture test]

### Smart lists
- **R-PAR-27** Lists are personal (owner only) or team (members read; Owner/Admin/TL write). Team lists carry no free-text clauses (no names, phones or emails typed into a shared definition). [DB, smoke + core]
- **R-PAR-28** A list is a definition, never a stored result. Each run is under the viewer's own RLS and returns only people whose **detail** the viewer sees. Detail-only predicates (tags, fields, tasks, opt-out facets) are `EXISTS` subqueries on RLS-protected tables, so a pond card cannot be found by data its viewer cannot see. [DB, smoke + api per-role test]
- **R-PAR-29** Definitions are validated by a core Zod schema (v1: AND of up to 20 clauses, plus at most one OR group of up to 10) and compiled only to whitelisted, parameterised Kysely fragments (lint rule + fuzz test; no `sql.raw` with input). Keyset pagination, 50 per page; counts are exact to 10,000, then "10,000+"; statement timeout 3 s (`LIST_TOO_SLOW`). Budget: first page p95 ≤ 400 ms, with count ≤ 800 ms, at 250k contacts / 1M tag links / 2M field values, for an Owner and for an Agent owning 2k, as a seeded CI perf test. [api perf test]

### Bulk actions
- **R-PAR-33** One person creates, previews and confirms a bulk action, as themselves and in their current role. The record holds that role. Only the creator writes a draft's content (an Admin cannot put words in an agent's mouth) and, in that same role, confirms. The confirmed count must equal the frozen items, so every cap holds on the real number. The lifecycle is draft → confirmed → running → completed/failed, with cancel from any live state. Kind, creator, source and parameters never change. Create, confirm, cancel and complete are audited with counts (no PII). Job payloads hold only the action id. [DB, smoke + api]
- **R-PAR-34** The target is built when the draft is created, from people whose detail the creator sees then, and confirmed exactly as previewed; nothing is ever added after confirm. There is no re-preview loop when a list grows. An item keeps its person and address, and a processed item is final (no re-send). The runner processes one item per transaction **as the creator**, rebuilt from the row while their membership is active, as `dispatchOutbound` rebuilds a human sender. RLS and `can()` therefore re-decide each item. An item no longer visible, merged or deleted is `skipped` with its reason. A suspended creator stops the run. [DB, smoke + api]
- **R-PAR-35** Per kind:
  - **tag/untag/stage**: as `contact.edit`, with R-PAR-14 per item.
  - **assign**: Owner/Admin/TL (within their team) only, one ownership command per person with a derived command id (idempotent), R-OWN-18 checks, no SLA. Per-person notices are replaced by one summary per affected user, the agents whose offers it withdrew included ("1 lead offered to you was withdrawn: it was assigned to someone else"). Before Confirm, closed people are left out (`CLOSED`: the engine assigns no closed lead) and the people on offer to an agent now are counted ("assigning withdraws that offer, and the agent is told"; batch 6 review F6).
  - **enroll**: approved version only; `ALREADY_ENROLLED` and unreachable people are skipped.
  - TCs run none.
  [DB CHECK + core + api]
- **R-PAR-36** Every bulk send is one `messages` row per person through `dispatchOutbound`. `canContact` and both DB opt-out guards apply to each message with no bulk exemption, so an unsubscribe or STOP that arrives mid-run blocks the remaining messages. A bulk message must belong to a running action of its channel, sent by its creator, to a person whose item is still pending, at exactly the address frozen in it (`messages_bulk_guard`). A client reply between queue and dispatch cancels that item (`CLIENT_REPLIED`). [DB, smoke + api]
- **R-PAR-37** A bulk message is never a touch. TX1 writes no touch when `bulk_action_id` is set, `v_agent_activity_daily` excludes bulk messages, and a message never loses its `bulk_action_id`. The scoreboard's own counts (`app.scoreboard_activity` and its daily rollup) exclude bulk messages too, and in the Priority Feed a bulk message never answers a waiting client. Speed-to-lead and the untouched-lead exception are unaffected. [DB view + definers, smoke + dispatch + api test]
- **R-PAR-38** One send per address per action: spouses sharing an email get one email, and the preview says "2 people share an address". [DB, smoke]
- **R-PAR-39** A bulk email is always marketing: unsubscribe link, one-click headers, brokerage name and postal address. The preflight refuses the action without a verified sending domain and the brokerage postal address. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL** (CAN-SPAM footer wording). [DB CHECK, smoke + api]
- **R-PAR-40** Batch text (bulk SMS) is **off by default**. Only the Owner turns it on, after re-auth and a typed attestation recorded in `audit_log`. The settings page says plainly that D1 removed consent checks, and that some state laws (for example Florida's FTSA and Oklahoma's telephone solicitation act) regulate automated selection or dialing of marketing texts, require prior express written consent, and limit hours and frequency, none of which this product checks. Owner or Admin may turn it off. A batch text reaches at most 200 people, needs an approved sender registration whose use case covers marketing, and ends with "Reply STOP to opt out". The switch is re-read at every message, so turning it off stops a running batch. **VERIFY WITH COUNSEL before any tenant enables it** (counsel item C-PAR-1). [DB, smoke + api]
- **R-PAR-41** A skipped or blocked bulk item writes the normal blocked-send `gate_decisions` row (for sends) but raises no `failed_send` exception (as R-MKT-8). The creator gets one `bulk_finished` notification with counts by reason ("Emailed 398 of 412: 9 unsubscribed, 5 no email"). [api]
- **R-PAR-42** Cancel stops pending items and cancels queued, not-yet-dispatching messages. Done items stay done (no "unsend"), and undoing a tag or stage change is a new bulk action. [api]

### Import
- **R-PAR-46** The FUB/CSV import extension, inside the existing contacts stage:
  - **Stages**: the dry run lists distinct FUB stage labels with counts, and an Admin maps each to one of the eight keys (`import_batches.options.stageMap`; changing it resets dry runs). The option `keepStageAsTag` adds the tag `FUB stage: <label>`.
  - **Tags**: upserted into the catalog before contacts are written (first spelling wins; `TAG_LIMIT` reported).
  - **Custom fields**: FUB custom fields map to new or existing typed definitions. A value that does not fit is an issue, and its raw text stays in `imported_fields`.
  - **Existing contacts**: an import never overwrites a value an agent set (E41 fill-empty).
  - **Revert**: removes exactly the batch's rows unless they were edited since.
  - No FUB tag ever acts as anything but a tag, except the existing opt-out tag conversion.
  [core + api e2e]

## 14. FUB parity edge cases

| # | Case | Handling | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| E162 | Agent @mentions an ISA whose collaborator window expired | The ISA is not notified; the author sees "can't see Jane" with Share, at save | R-PAR-2 |
| E163 | Mentioned TL loses the lead (reassigned) after being notified | Opening the notification is a 404 "You no longer have access"; the notification carried no text | R-PAR-2, R-PAR-3 |
| E164 | Author edits a note and adds a second mention | Only the new person is checked and notified (per-user dedupe key) | R-PAR-3 |
| E165 | Agent types "buyer" while "Buyer" exists | The existing tag is used; display stays "Buyer" | R-PAR-6 |
| E166 | Admin merges "Hot" into "A-Lead" while an agent tags someone "Hot" | One transaction: the agent's insert waits or fails on the deleted tag and the UI retries with the target | R-PAR-9 |
| E167 | Agent tags someone "DNC" | The tag is saved, and the app offers the real do-not-contact; nothing is blocked by the tag | R-PAR-7 |
| E168 | Agent removes the mobile that texted STOP, then texts the work phone | Refused `RECIPIENT_OPTED_OUT`; the UI names the removed number | R-PAR-12 |
| E169 | STOP came from a wrong number on the record | Record wrong number (call outcome or reply, which writes the ledger), then remove as wrong number; the real phone becomes textable, and the wrong number itself stays blocked | R-PAR-12 |
| E170 | Agent "fixes" a typo in a phone | Remove (typo) + add; the old number's history stays with the old HMAC | R-PAR-11 |
| E171 | Agent moves an untouched routed lead from `new` to `nurture` (or `lost`) | `STAGE_NEEDS_CONTACT`; Close is offered instead | R-PAR-14 |
| E172 | Stage changes to `active` and two playbooks would auto-enroll (team + agent) | The agent's own playbook wins for their contacts, else the team's; one live enrollment, never two | R-PAR-16 |
| E173 | Two Admins add the 50th and 51st custom field at once | Advisory lock; the second gets `CUSTOM_FIELD_LIMIT` | R-PAR-17 |
| E174 | Admin renames option "Pre-approved" to "Preapproved" | Values reference the option id; only the label changes | R-PAR-17 |
| E175 | Field labelled "Military status" | Refused (`CUSTOM_FIELD_PROTECTED`, and the built-in key CHECK); the sheet points to the self-reported attribute | R-PAR-18 |
| E176 | Birthday on 29 February | "Coming up" uses 28 February in non-leap years | R-PAR-20 |
| E177 | Agent sees Alice but not her husband (another agent's lead) | No relationship row is visible; no count | R-PAR-21 |
| E178 | Erasing one spouse | Their relationship rows, tags, values and stage history cascade; the other spouse is untouched | R-PAR-21 |
| E179 | A first name contains `{{agent.email}}` | Inserted literally (one pass) | R-PAR-24 |
| E180 | Team template edited while a bulk email using it runs | The running action uses its frozen snapshot | R-PAR-26 |
| E181 | Agent runs the TL's team list | Gets only their own people; the page says "Showing people you can see" | R-PAR-28 |
| E182 | A pond card's tags would match a list filter | Not returned: lists return only detail-visible people | R-PAR-28 |
| E183 | Agent bulk-emails 300; 20 are reassigned away mid-run | Those items `skipped NOT_VISIBLE`; the new owner's people are not emailed by the old agent | R-PAR-34 |
| E184 | A selected person unsubscribes during the run | Their item is `blocked RECIPIENT_OPTED_OUT` by the DB guard, with no override | R-PAR-36 |
| E185 | Owner turns batch text off mid-run | The next text item is refused `BATCH_TEXT_DISABLED`; the rest are skipped with that reason | R-PAR-40 |
| E186 | The list grew between preview and confirm | The action confirms exactly the people it previewed; the new ones are not included (a new action picks them up) | R-PAR-33, R-PAR-34 |
| E187 | Bulk assign of 200 to an agent at capacity | The engine decides per item; rejections land in `ownership_command_rejections` and the item is `skipped` with the engine's code | R-PAR-35 |
| E188 | Worker crash mid-chunk | The item's transaction rolls back and stays `pending`; messages already `dispatching` follow the stale-dispatch sweeper (never re-sent) | R-PAR-34 |
| E189 | A list audience uses the protected tag "VA buyer" for a bulk email | Refused `AUDIENCE_PROTECTED_PROXY`; viewing the list still works | R-PAR-8 |
| E190 | FUB import brings 1,400 distinct tags | The dry run reports `TAG_LIMIT`; the Admin merges or skips; the raw list stays in `imported_fields` | R-PAR-46 |
| E191 | Two FUB stages map to `nurture` and the team wants them distinct | `keepStageAsTag` keeps each as a tag | R-PAR-46 |
| E192 | A logged call is dated tomorrow | Refused (`notes_activity_at_window`) | R-PAR-31 |
| E193 | Agent logs "they said stop" in free text only | Nothing is blocked; the sheet asks "Did they ask not to be contacted?" with the button | R-PAR-32 |
| E194 | A saved list holds `{ kind: 'stage', in: [...] }` and the team then uses "Stage is not Closed, Lost" | Exclusions are new union members (`not_in` on stage, source, owner, intent, contact type); the definition stays `v: 1`, every saved definition parses and compiles to the same plan as before (golden plans), `in` and `not_in` together are refused | R-PAR-29 |
| E195 | "Source is not Zillow" and some people have no source | Kept: a person with no source is not from Zillow. `"is"` never matches a missing value, so "is" and "is not" split the people the viewer sees exactly in two (VERIFY against FUB) | R-PAR-29 |
| E196 | "Intent is not Buyer" and some people have no intent | Kept, the same rule as E195 (stage and contact type are never empty) | R-PAR-29 |
| E197 | "Owner is not Avery" and some people are unassigned (pond) | Kept for the viewers who may see that person in detail (Owner, Admin, Team Lead); an agent still sees only their own people. A person has no owner when the engine says so: unassigned, in a pond, on offer to an agent, or closed, and the builder names the "no owner" clause for all four ("Owner: nobody (unassigned, in a pond, on offer or closed)"; batch 6 review F6); a bulk Assign from such a list says what happens to the people on offer and leaves the closed ones out (R-PAR-35) | R-PAR-28, R-PAR-29 |
| E198 | An exclusion list with an unknown stage, a malformed id, 51 ids, or 21 clauses | Refused `422 LIST_DEFINITION_INVALID` / `LIST_DEFINITION_TOO_LARGE` with the path; never a 500; the values reach SQL only as bound parameters | R-PAR-29 |
| E199 | An agent filters "Owner is Bob" or "Owner is not me", or a definition names another tenant's source or user | Returns (and counts) only people the agent may see in detail: 0, never Bob's book; a foreign id matches nobody. The builder's catalogs (`GET /people/filter-options`) are the tenant's own sources and members, names only | R-PAR-28, R-TEN-3 |
| E200 | A Team Lead filters by name ("jane") inside Active and saves it as a team list | Refused before the tap and by the server: "Team lists can't include typed text. Remove the name search or save it as your own list." Saved as their own list it works; a bulk send to any filtered list still skips every opted-out person (D1) | R-PAR-27, R-PAR-36 |
| E230 | The member a FUB user is mapped to is suspended between the ownership approval and its commit | Owner decision B4 (2026-11-11). Nothing is assigned to them: the engine refuses the target (`TARGET_NOT_ELIGIBLE`), and the people mapped to them stay as the contacts stage left them, unassigned and never routed, with no offer, SLA job or notice. Each record is not imported for a documented reason (`OWNER_UNAVAILABLE`, naming the member and why: "Brought in unassigned: Blake Chen's account is suspended."). The recount applies the documented outcomes to the approved counts (exactly that many move from imported to rejected), so the stage verifies; the verification shows and stores both. The later stages commit. The reason is said in the present only while the member's membership still shows it; once it does not (the member was reactivated), it is said as it was at the commit ("Blake Chen's account was suspended when the owners were imported"), and the verification always says it so (review B4-R2). Not an owner decision: the implementation handles every other reason the engine refuses an import target the same way (a member no longer active, a role that holds no leads), each record naming that reason | R-IMPORT-5, R-IMPORT-8 |
| E231 | The Owner gives those people an owner after the migration | The ownership report lists them (who, why, and where each is now: still unassigned, given an owner, in a pond, on offer, closed, deleted or merged into another contact; "has an owner" only of someone owned, review B4-R1) and offers "Assign these people": the existing bulk Assign with exactly the ids of those still unassigned selected (at most 2,000 per action). Owner and Admin can; a Team Lead reaches no unassigned person (R-OWN-18); an agent cannot bulk-assign (`BULK_NOT_PERMITTED`); the suspended member is never a target (`NOT_FOUND`). Each assignment is the engine's ordinary `assigned` event by that person. The report also lists the people this import created who were merged with another person, or whose holder someone changed, after the contacts were imported and whom nobody holds ("Brought in unassigned: they were merged with another person after the contacts were imported.", batch 6 review F3), one line per person (two records a merge joined are one), and links a person merged away since to the record they live on now (review F7). Where they are now is said once, by the people card, next to the button it names (review F8). One of them who asks again is routed like a returning lead (owner decision D8, E341): their line says when they asked (the inquiry's time, in the team's time: one who asked before the owners came in was routed once they had, E343) and where the routing took them ("Asked again on Nov 12, 2026, 3:04 PM EST and was offered to Avery Stone.", or "…and was put in Team Pond."), the category's lines say how many ("2 of them asked again since they were imported and were routed by your routing rules."), and "Assign these people" no longer selects them once they are on offer, in a pond or owned | R-PAR-35 |
| E232 | The member is reactivated before the ownership commit, or suspended while it runs | Whether a member can hold leads is decided at the commit, so a member reactivated before it gets their people normally. While it runs, each chunk re-reads FOR SHARE the members it assigned a lead to, just before it commits: a suspension that committed meanwhile makes the chunk run again (those people come in unassigned, E230), and a suspension asked for after the re-read waits for the chunk. No import assignment ever commits after its member's suspension did | R-IMPORT-4, R-IMPORT-8 |
| E233 | The team's Follow Up Boss account holds tasks and appointments | Owner decision B7 (2026-11-11). They are not imported. The connection test and the snapshot count them from one-record count pages (`_metadata.total`; never a list, never a record; an export file's own lists are counted), and the Imports page (from the connection test on) and the final report (once something has been imported, review F3) say: "Follow Up Boss has 1,240 tasks and 85 appointments. Realty OS doesn't bring tasks or appointments over: finish or note the open ones in Follow Up Boss before you switch." A count that cannot be read (no scope, an error, a file without them) is said so and never guessed. One the snapshot cannot read again keeps the earlier number with the step that counted it, said as such ("we could not count its tasks again (it had 3 tasks at the connection test)"), never as the snapshot's or as what Follow Up Boss holds now (review F4). Counting never fails the connection test or the snapshot and never blocks a stage. The FUB endpoints are VERIFY and configurable (`FUB_TASKS_PATH`, `FUB_APPOINTMENTS_PATH`, one plain path segment). A CSV file has neither, so nothing is said | R-IMPORT-1 |
